Robert Pattinson's James Bond Imagined In Gun-Slinging 007 Art

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Some moviegoers simply aren't waiting for Robert Pattinson's James Bond to arrive in theaters.

After much speculation by fans and the press and an intense casting race, it was announced on Monday of last week that Pattinson will be the new Bond in the upcoming Bond 26 movie from Amazon MGM Studios, with Denis Villeneuve in the director's chair. While Villeneuve is only confirmed to be a part of one Bond movie, Pattinson has already signed on for at least three.

The studio released a very short teaser featuring Pattinson in character to announce his casting. But unaffiliated creators are also at work, such as @cole on Instagram, who shared three concept art pieces of Pattinson as Bond on November 17. One image depicts Pattinson as Bond looking through a bullet hole; another shows him standing next to a vintage car for a mock Hollywood Reporter cover.

Check out @cole's fan art of Robert Pattinson as James Bond below:

"Will Robert Pattinson be a better #JamesBond than Daniel Craig??" the creator ponders in the post's caption. Pattinson will be the seventh actor to play Ian Fleming's iconic British spy for the screen, with Daniel Craig being the most recent predecessor, starring in five Bond movies from 2006 to 2021. The other five previous Bond actors are Sean Connery, George Lazenby, Roger Moore, Timothy Dalton, and Pierce Brosnan.

Bond 26 will mark Pattinson's second collaboration with acclaimed director Villeneuve; their first project together, Dune: Part Three, will hit theaters next month. Pattinson is a newcomer to the cast for this installment, while Villeneuve also directed Dune and Dune: Part Two (both receiving Best Picture nominations), as well as Arrival and Blade Runner 2049 before that.

Pattinson, meanwhile, is coming off a strong streak of movies, including Mickey 17, The Drama, The Odyssey, Primetime, and The Batman. The Twilight actor was chosen for the role of James Bond over peers including Callum Turner, Josh O'Connor, and Jack Lowden. Both Pattinson and Villeneuve have distinct creative styles, and the world will now have to wait to see how they will make Bond 26 their own.

Meanwhile, other creators speculate about what that vision could look like in works of fan art. @cole draws upon classic Bond imagery, but different artists and even the filmmakers themselves could come up with something more unconventional in the months to come.

Bond 26 is reported to release theatrically in 2028.

7 Near-Perfect Showtime Series With 10 Episodes Or Fewer

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Since the age of streaming, Showtime has found itself outpaced by HBO, and its streaming service efforts were short-lived and have, at this point, been completely subsumed by its parent Paramount+. There are still original shows under the Showtime banner, however, and these seven are worth subscribing for.

The Showtime miniseries Picture Windows premiered in 1995 and ran for six episodes. An anthology, each episode of Picture Windows is a short film based on a different iconic painting. There's one based on Primavera by Sandro Botticelli, another based on Ballet Rehearsal by Edgar Degas, and so on.

Each episode also features a notable person behind the camera. Peter Bogdanovich, Joe Dante, and John Boorman are credited in the series. It's a beautiful, thoughtful miniseries, and though 30 minutes may sound like not enough time these days, it allows for sharp, quick-moving stories.

It's a series filled with some impressive stars as well, with Alan Arkin, Sally Kirkland, Robert Loggia, Steve Zahn, and John Hurt all making appearances. It only takes about three hours to watch, but it's also the kind of show that makes you pause after each episode and consider everything you've seen.

Out of Order is a six-episode miniseries from Donna and Wayne Powers about a man, Mark Colm (Eric Stoltz), who is in an unsatisfying marriage with Lorna (Felicity Huffman). Kim Dickens, William H. Macy, and Justine Bateman also star. The series was edited into a feature film for the DVD.

Mark decides that his midlife crisis will involve a woman named Danni (Dickens) and imagining his life as a sort of reality show. Lorna ends up with Steven (Macy) and begins drinking a lot. There's more to the series than that, though, and it ends up being a moving story of love lost and gained.

There's a meta quality to the series that may divide viewers, but the story is told with such brutal honesty that even if Out of Order's quirks are not your cup of tea, you'll be swept up in the tale. It takes a nuanced approach to relationships and has empathy for everyone involved.

Escape at Dannemora is a seven-episode miniseries based on the real-life 2015 Clinton Correctional Facility escape. Patricia Arquette stars as Joyce "Tilly" Mitchell, a prison worker at Clinton who is in a loveless marriage and is having an affair with David Sweat (Paul Dano), a convicted murderer.

Her relationship with Sweat eventually results in her willingness to help Sweat and another convicted murderer, Richard Matt (Benicio del Toro), escape Clinton, with disastrous consequences for all involved. The series begins with Tilly's interrogation after the manhunt is over, and backtracks.

The series takes its time to set up all the pieces that lead to the main event, and for a show with a promised prison break, there is a lot more character work than you may initially expect. It's all critical, though, and when the escape does happen, the groundwork that goes into the beginning of the show pays off.

Patrick Melrose is a five-episode series starring Benedict Cumberbatch as the wealthy, titular Englishman, Patrick Melrose. The series tracks five decades of Patrick's life, starting in the 1960s and ending in the 2000s. Over this time, Patrick attempts to overcome the demons imparted to him by a childhood of parental abuse.

The series uses flashbacks to help us understand Patrick's difficult upbringing and also tear down the tapestry of upper-class British society. Based on Edward St Aubyn's semi-autobiographical novels, Patrick Melrose is unflinching in its look at childhood trauma and its reverberations felt decades later.

It's a powerful series, with a moving ending that may not exactly satisfy, but is the right choice for such a hard show. It's a scathing look at aristocracy, and is unafraid to point the finger at societal problems as much as it is at personal problems. Cumberbatch is doing some of his best work as the complicated, haunted Melrose.

The Good Lord Bird is a seven-episode Western series based on the 2013 novel of the same name by James McBride. The series is based on the real-life exploits of John Brown, an abolitionist during Bleeding Kansas, whose anti-slavery efforts eventually resulted in the famous raid on Harper's Ferry.

Joshua Caleb Johnson stars as Henry "Onion" Shackleford, a fictional enslaved boy who escapes his captors and joins Brown's (Ethan Hawke) retinue. Onion comes to trust and believe in the eccentric John Brown, even as he appears to become more erratic over the years, and his fight against slavery more desperate.

It's a masterclass performance from Hawke, who imbues Brown with a supernatural fury and religious fervor that might make him seem crazy to a modern audience, but is a symbol of his disgust and revulsion at the practice of slavery. It's a thrilling, even fun series, with sobering moments that hit you hard in the chest.

Fellow Travelers is a seven-episode series based on Thomas Mallon's 2007 novel of the same name. Matt Bomer and Jonathan Bailey star as Hawk Fuller and Tim Laughlin, respectively, two men who begin a romance at the height of McCarthyism in America, tracing their relationship over the decades.

From Adolescence to Mare of Easttown to the classic Roots, these incredible TV miniseries didn't need a lot of episodes to make their point.

Hawk is a World War II veteran and State Department official who is terrified of being outed, while Tim is a devout Catholic and hard-line supporter of McCarthy whose worldview is suddenly flipped upside down after his encounter with Hawk. Their relationship winds through the tumultuous '60s and beyond.

It's an epic and full depiction of gay romance, including everything from Fire Island to the AIDs epidemic, and each moment in history is brought to life, often sadly, with the incredible performances of Bomer and Bailey, who share a moving chemistry. It's an expansive series that uses Hawk and Tim's beautiful relationship to personalize a story we don't see often enough.

A Gentleman in Moscow is a Paramount+ co-production that runs eight episodes and is based on Amor Towles' 2016 novel. Ewan McGregor stars as Count Alexander Ilyich Rostov, an aristocrat who returns to Russia from Paris after being exiled following the events of the October Revolution.

As an aristocrat, Alexander is under constant surveillance from the Russian state, but over the decades, he manages to make a life for himself despite the rules and restrictions placed on him. He meets and falls in love with actress Anna Urbanova (Mary Elizabeth Winstead), and the two attempt to survive the upheaval in the USSR.

McGregor and Winstead provide a winning combo, one that we saw once before in Fargo, but is much sweeter this time around. It's a beautiful show, where the history is secondary to the relationship between the pair, and it makes for one of Showtime's most moving, short shows.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Spain's Sanchez makes final appeal to voters before election

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Spanish Prime Minister Pedro Sanchez told people to turn out in large numbers so the country does not go "backwards." Polls suggest an end to his eight-year leftist leadership of Spain.

Socialist Prime Minister Pedro Sanchez on Friday hit the campaign trail one last time before Spaniards head to the polls on Sunday, telling people to "vote, vote, vote" to make sure the country doesn't go "backwards."

Opinion polls point to a change in government, with the conservative People's Party (PP) slated to come out on top, bringing an end to Sanchez's eight years of leftist leadership.

Spain's general election this Sunday is also crucial because it kicks off a round of elections on the continentand because it is yet another test of the region's shift to the right.

The PP would potentially have to rely on support from the far-right anti-immigration Vox party to form the next government in Spain.

There's also a chance that the election ends in a hung parliament, with neither the right-wing coalition nor the left bloc securing a majority.

Sanchez has so far campaigned on a platform of having boosted the Spanish economy when its European Union partners were struggling.

He has pointed to his policies of having increased the minimum wage and having lifted pensions in line with inflation.

But people are frustrated that the cost of living has soared and that they're not able to enjoy the fruits of economic growth, with many unable to leave their parents' house because of very high rental prices.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Sanchez said during his final rally in Madrid that the "elections are about moving Spain forward, not moving it backwards."

He doubled down on an attempt to appeal to left-leaning voters, telling them that a right coalition government, with far-right members in parliament, would reverse the years of social progress and gut public services.

Oscar-winning Spanish film director Pedro Almodovar was also at the rally this evening.

Sanchez wore a red T-shirt that said "the power of the people," telling people that a "progressive majority is within our reach."

Sanchez has also become the standard-bearer for Europeans frustrated with the Trump administration.

PP leader Alberto Nunez Feijoo, who has said that he wants to maintain good relations with US President Donald Trump, dismissed Sanchez's campaign of "fear."

Feijoo won the most seats during elections in 2023, but he was not able to form a government.

"And when a government has nothing to offer but fear, it means it has nothing else left to offer," he told supporters at his campaign finale in the southern city of Seville.

"Governing is about managing, and I have spent my whole life preparing for this moment," said Feijoo, who built his career running central public bodies and the northwestern Galicia region.

He has advocated for tough immigration policies and stronger border management.

Santiago Abascal, the leader of the Vox party, favors a much tougher approach, with his party having advocated for mass deportations.

Addressing a closing rally at a bullring in the eastern city of Valencia, Abascal told supporters not to "vote out of fear. Vote with courage."

"What we are up against is a criminal mafia led by a ruthless boss, a liar," he said, referring to the corruption scandals that have surrounded Sanchez and his government.

Travis Barker Shares Sweet Way Son Rocky, 3, is His Literal Mini-Me

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Travis Barker revealed just how alike he and wife Kourtney Kardashian's 3-year-old Rocky are, sharing that the two share a fondness for a very specific accessory.

Travis Barker is thankful for all the small things he shares with Rocky Barker.

Especially as the Blink-182 drummer revealed that he and wife Kourtney Kardashian’s 3-year-old son share the exact same taste in Vans sneakers.

“We have a version of my black Dues Paid and a version of my white ones that are all studded out and he has ‘em too,” Barker shared in a recent interview with Complex. “And it’s his go-tos.”

And Rocky has made no secret of his penchant for his dad’s style of shoe.

“You ask him to pick any shoe in his closet—mind you, he’s 3—but he’ll say the spiked ones. ‘I want the spiked ones,’” Barker added. “And everywhere we go, people are like, ‘Oh look at his shoes.’”

The 51-year-old—who is stepdad to Atiana de la Hoya, 27, and shares Landon Barker, 22, and Alabama Barker, 20, with ex Shanna Moakler—quipped that the studded shoes are definitely, “so hard. It’s so tough.”

And the similarities between Barker and Rocky don’t stop at their shoes. Rocky also loves being the toddler at a rock show, as Kardashian shared a video of her son rocking out to Blink-182 during a February concert in Arizona.

In the clip posted to Instagram, Rocky could be seen bopping along backstage as Barker played the drums in the distance.

And it’s certainly a special time for Rocky, who celebrated his third birthday earlier this month with an epic Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles birthday party.

Kardashian—also mom to kids Mason, 16, Penelope, 14, and Reign, 11, with ex Scott Disick—gave fans a look at the most excellent celebrations, posting a video to her Instagram Stories as the family made their way through an elaborate “sewer” that had green slime oozing all around before opening up to the backyard.

The Poosh founder also shared a heartwarming birthday tribute to Rocky on Instagram Nov. 12, posting two photos of the tiny tot as a newborn baby.

She captioned the sweet snaps, “Heaven blew every trumpet and played every horn on the wonderful, marvelous night you were born. 11.1.23.”

HBO’s 6-Part Miniseries Is Still One of the Best Ever Made

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Molly DeCastro is a New York-based emerging film and entertainment journalist with a personal passion for poetic prose and impactful storytelling. She graduated from Pace University with a degree in English and a concentration in creative writing. While film analysis is at the heart of her work, she is especially interested in exploring cinema through an anthropological lens and in examining how creative themes reflect cultural identity. She aims to forge a deep connection between audiences and film through thoughtful and evocative writing. She also enjoys keeping up with all things film! That means specializing in industry news, emerging directors, and stand-out projects.

I Know This Much Is True, adapted from Wally Lamb’s 1998 bestselling novel, follows the fractured yet mirrored lives of identical twin brothers. Starring Mark Ruffalo as both Dominik and Thomas Birdsey, the story centers on the aftermath of crisis and the relentless passage of time and age. Their bond, despite its strain, somehow captures the inescapable weight of generational trauma and the confusing yet undying devotion found between brothers. Director Derek Cianfrance paints a beautiful, tragic story, showing with immense care and depth the tenderness of the human experience.

In short, the audience meets the Birdsey brothers at a sore spot in their lives. Thomas, a lifelong paranoid schizophrenic, in a very public and violent fashion, protests the Gulf War by sacrificing his own hand. Consequently, he’s thrust into the legal system with only his brother Dominik on the outside to vouch for him. All at once, he must go from being a brother to the sole advocate and protector, all the while unearthing his own trauma as his story unfolds.

Ruffalo's claim to fame is, of course, tied to Marvel. Because of this, it can be easy to forget what a truly stellar performer he is. Critics and fans alike have pointed to his performance of Thomas and Dominik Birdsey as the most emotionally raw and resonant of his entire career. Each character demands something entirely different. Each is incredibly complex, nuanced, and requires an incredible level of adaptability and grace to pull off. Ruffalo appears to balance both Thomas's schizophrenia and Dominik’s grief effortlessly.

While scenes with himself are incredible, so are scenes with his cast mates. Alongside Kathryn Hahn as Dessa, Dominik's ex-wife, or Rosie O'Donnell as Thomas's social worker, sometimes clashing with them in these big, loud, cross-cutting conflicts or sharing these quiet, near-whispered intimate moments, with such a high level of skill captured on screen, there is so much room for play and for depth. Much of the series is heavy, some have said, exhaustively so. However, the performances tugging it along are most certainly worth a watch.

As is often the case when a series comes from a book, the plot of this show is truly incredible. The care and poignancy are evident from the very beginning. The Bridsey brothers were born into a family with many, many dark secrets. Each of them bears the weight. Their mother is a submissive, exploited woman, controlled by their abusive stepfather. He had a particular affinity for abusing Thomas as well, claiming he was soft and often asking for punishment. Notably, for the sake of their protection, she never revealed their birth father’s identity.

Present day, decades later, Dominic is a bit shifty but relatively stable in comparison to Thomas, who developed schizophrenia and fell in and out of institutions for years. Through creating a past so tight and intricately woven, their present becomes all the more deep. Each of their motives becomes entirely intuitive to the audience, and thus one not only feels for the Birdsey boys, but feels deeply connected to them. As the story progresses, the desperation grows thicker. Things unravel rather quickly. Dominik is unrelenting in his pursuit to vindicate his brother, and Thomas is entirely reliant on his persistence. The flashbacks and winding plots within the series are both truly sickening and incredibly loving. That combination makes for a truly thrilling experience.

Affleck's movie sees a mayoral candidate whose son is kidnapped.

The series achieved both commercial and critical success. Ruffalo won an Emmy, as well as a Golden Globe, and a Screen Actors Guild Award. It holds a highly decorated 74% on Rotten Tomatoes, and by all accounts is an incredible must-watch. There are very few series that result in unanimous approval, and This Much I Know Is True earned exactly that. From the plot to the performances to the original novel itself, each piece of the puzzle created a highly decorated, well-loved masterwork.

Following the parallel lives of identical twin brothers Dominick and Thomas Birdsey in a story of betrayal, sacrifice, and forgiveness.

10 Spies More Lethal Than James Bond

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Few literary spies are as synonymous with danger and lethality as James Bond. Ian Fleming’s 007 literally has a license to kill, proving that a capacity for violence isn’t just part of his job description, it’s one of his greatest professional strengths. Across Fleming’s 14 James Bond books and the dozens of continuations from other authors that followed, M16’s most famous spy permanently dispatched countless foes in a variety of painful and creative ways.

James Bond may be the poster child for dangerous fictional spies, but in reality there are plenty who make him look relatively tame. The Bond of the novels is far less trigger-happy than his movie counterpart, but the same can’t be said for the generations of thriller spies he inspired. Plenty are far more lethal than 007, with the most dangerous completing missions through means Fleming’s vision of Her Majesty’s Secret Service would never allow.

Surviving the massacre of a presidential protection detail is an unusually brutal introduction to a thriller protagonist. That’s precisely how readers encounter Scot Harvath in Brad Thor’s The Lions of Lucerne, which establishes that his character has much more depth than his Secret Service credentials. Before protecting presidents, Harvath served as a Navy SEAL. His subsequent career in covert counterterrorism after the death of his security team combines both backgrounds, creating an operative who understands how protection works and possesses the military skills to dismantle it.

Throughout his series, Scot Harvath fights multiple sustained campaigns of violence under conditions that should finish him. For example, 2019’s Backlash strips away his support and leaves him fighting through hostile Russian territory, turning his survival skills and appetite for revenge into an especially vicious combination. It’s not uncommon for Harvath to kill dozens of people in a single novel. This is especially true when his friends and family are at risk and things get personal. Bond is dangerous when cornered, but Harvath’s special operations background makes pursuing him an exceptionally costly mistake.

You can purchase The Lions of Lucerne, the first Scot Harvath novel by Brad Thor, here.

An art restorer hardly sounds like the obvious choice to outclass 007. The civilian profession of author Daniel Silva’s Gabriel Allon, however, suits a killer whose defining qualities include patience, precision, and extraordinary attention to detail. Recruited into Israeli intelligence after the 1972 Munich Olympics massacre, Allon participated in the campaign to eliminate those responsible. His background establishes him as a specialist in targeted assassination long before the first novel in his series, 2000’s The Kill Artist, begins, with the consequences of that work following him throughout Daniel Silva’s novels.

Gabriel Allon is a notably lethal spy when he’s dealing with his targets up close. Daniel Silva describes his early assassinations as close-range shootings with a .22-caliber Beretta, emphasizing nerve and preparation over overwhelming firepower. He’s not a literary spy that only has violence to rely on, however. Allon’s linguistic abilities and talent for assuming convincing identities make reaching the target part of the same skill set as killing them. Whereas meeting James Bond isn’t an encounter those in his orbit forget in a hurry, Gabriel Allon knows how to become completely unremarkable until he makes his move. The fact that various organizations try to bring him back to his career as an assassin despite him wanting to leave that life behind is further testament to just how lethal he truly is.

You can purchase The Kill Artist, the first Gabriel Allon novel by Daniel Silva, here.

Losing his memory should make Jason Bourne an easier target. Instead, 1980’s The Bourne Identity introduced readers to a covert operative whose survival instincts and skills remain terrifyingly effective even after he’s forgotten honing them. Author Robert Ludlum builds much of the suspense of the Jason Bourne novels around that contradiction: the titular spy knows how to survive a world full of professional killers, but can’t remember how he acquired his lethal skillset. Bourne’s level of combat conditioning is so deep and thorough that his training kicks in on a subconscious level.

The novels also give Jason Bourne a considerably wider skillset than a straightforward assassin. Robert Ludlum’s original trilogy places Bourne in an elaborate operation that requires deception and infiltration just as much as armed and unarmed combat. Later authors expand the violence, placing Bourne against rival assassins and intelligence organizations equipped to anticipate his methods. Across those different incarnations, Bourne proves himself much more dangerous than 007 thanks to his incredible adaptability. Removing his support network, compromising his identity, or sending trained operatives after him never seems to make him any less of a threat.

You can purchase The Bourne Identity, the first Jason Bourne novel by Robert Ludlum, here.

The distinction between merely investigating terrorists and actively eliminating them is central to the appeal of the Mitch Rapp novels. Author Vince Flynn’s CIA operative specializes in the latter, approaching counterterrorism with a lethal urgency that repeatedly puts him at odds with his superiors. 2010’s American Assassin, which serves as a prequel, traces Rapp’s trigger-happy mentality back to personal loss and his recruitment into a covert assassination program. From there, Rapp honed himself into one of the most proficient killers at the CIA’s disposal.

What makes Mitch Rapp especially frightening is his cold and calculated approach to even the most violent operations. His training encompasses all manner of firearms and close combat, but it’s his willingness to use his skills without a second’s hesitation that makes them so deadly. This is where his operations differ significantly to 007’s, and this difference reveals everything about which is the more dangerous. James Bond’s assignments frequently require him to uncover a larger conspiracy before confronting its architect. Rapp is often dispatched because the U.S. Government wants an enemy to stop breathing.

You can purchase Transfer of Power, the first Mitch Rapp novel by Vince Flynn, here.

A body count only reveals so much about an assassin’s skills. Often how targets are killed matters just as much as how many have been dealt with. This makes John Rain an especially competent literary spy, since his victims often aren’t recognizable as murder victims at all. The Japanese American freelancer from Barry Eisler’s novels specializes in deaths that appear natural, making him particularly valuable to employers who need a person removed without attracting attention. Beginning with 2002’s Rain Fall, also published as A Clean Kill in Tokyo, Barry Eisler turns Rain’s ability to conceal violence the basis for one of the most lethal spies to grace the pages of a thriller novel.

As expected, the ability to make a murder look like an accident is far from the only trick up John Rain’s sleeve. Serving in the Special Forces during Vietnam and extensive martial arts experience mean he’s just as capable in an open fight as he is setting up an elaborate assassination. Still, it’s his penchant for creative and untraceable kills that define Rain. Surviving a gunfight is one thing. Arranging matters so that nobody realizes a killing occurred requires an entirely different level of control.

You can purchase A Clean Kill In Tokyo, the first John Rain novel by Barry Eisler, here.

Pike Logan belongs to an organization designed to operate beyond the normal limits of American law. In Brad Taylor’s novels, a black book group codenamed The Taskforce recruits from elite military and intelligence backgrounds. Even among a roster of highly experienced specialists, however, 2011’s One Rough Man establishes Logan as an especially lethal operative. His notably military approach makes him especially dangerous when a mission develops into a sustained confrontation.

Logan thinks about factors like surveillance, positioning, coordination, and escape as connected problems, and has no qualms about using lethal force whenever the situation demands it. Author Brad Taylor’s own Special Forces experience adds a notable level of detail that makes Logan’s professional competence feel legitimate. James Bond is an exceptional lone operative, but Pike Logan brings the tactical lethality and group-coordination of an elite military unit into the world of espionage. His lethality comes just as much from knowing how to use the people around him as it does any weapon.

You can purchase One Rough Man, the first Pike Logan novel by Brad Taylor, here.

Most fictional spies have a life before whatever intelligence service they work for recruits them. Even the enigmatic James Bond went to Eton and served in the Navy before joining Her Majesty’s Secret Service. Evan Smoak’s preparation begins at twelve, when the Orphan Program takes him from a group home and starts conditioning him to be a highly covert government assassin on a team that doesn’t officially exist. By adulthood, Smoak’s entire world has been defined by violence, surveillance, and operational discipline. Gregg Hurwitz’s Orphan X, the first novel in the series of the same name, introduces the result: a man whose formidable resources and specialized skills make his disappearance from the organization that made him a matter of national security.

Working as the Nowhere Man, Smoak redirects his abilities toward helping people, but that doesn’t make him any less dangerous to those who earn his attention. His lethality comes from obsessive preparation just as much as shooting or fighting. He assesses his surroundings, anticipates complications, and treats small mistakes as potential disasters, an approach that leads to watertight operation planning. James Bond is a man who has been trained for danger. Evan Smoak feels like he’s been psychologically conditioned into a living weapon.

You can purchase Orphan X, the first Evan Smoak novel by Gregg Hurwitz, here.

Being hunted by the CIA would be a serious problem for any literary spy, including James Bond. For Court Gentry, aka The Gray Man, it becomes an extended demonstration of why the agency couldn’t simply let him walk free after his years of service. Author Mark Greaney’s former covert assassin survives long after his own employers mark him for death. He manages to build a successful career as a freelancer despite being hunted by one of the most far-reaching intelligence organizations in the world.

As for why Court Gentry is more dangerous than James Bond, the central spy of The Gray Man novels has an advantage when it comes to sheer concentration of expertise. Unlike 007, who carries out a wide variety of types of missions, Gentry’s career is built on surviving encounters with other highly trained killers. Bond often comes face-to-face with dangerous foes, but this is rarely part of the plan. Gentry exists precisely to take out these kinds of threats. Bond may be the more well-rounded spy, but Gentry is inarguably the more lethal of the two.

You can purchase The Gray Man, the first Court Gentry novel by Mark Greaney, here.

Before adopting the name John Clark, John Kelly from the world of the Jack Ryan novels was a former Navy SEAL and Vietnam veteran whose personal revenge campaign develops alongside a military prisoner rescue operation. Tom Clancy gives Clark the skills for both undertakings when he made his debut in 1988’s The Cardinal of the Kremlin, and then spends multiple books showing just how ruthlessly he can apply them. Clark’s professional effectiveness comes from versatility accumulated over a long career. A potent combination of maritime, military, and clandestine fieldwork experience allows him to function across situations that demand very different kinds of expertise.

John Clark’s later leadership of the multinational counterterrorism unit Rainbow showcases the full extent of those capabilities, as he turns a team of elite but disconnected specialists into a lethally coherent unit. James Bond may have formidable instincts when violence becomes the only option, but Clark’s professional capacity for it ensures that he rarely, if ever, needs to rely on anything but carefully applied brute force. He understands how to plan a mission, select the necessary people, and deliver operational success even when up against overwhelming firepower.

You can purchase The Cardinal of the Kremlin, the first novel to feature John Clark by Tom Clancy, here.

Espionage missions that heavily feature luxury, global travel, and impeccable presentation make Villanelle, aka Oxana Vorontsova, an obvious spiritual successor to James Bond. However, she doesn’t just build on his legacy when it comes to making the spy lifestyle look lavish. She also embodies the cold-blooded professional killer archetype that Ian Fleming’s 007 is often mistaken as being. Author Luke Jennings introduces Vorontsova in the Killing Eve novels as a Russian killer recruited by the secretive Twelve, whose intervention transforms an already violent young woman into an international assassin. Her handlers supply new identities and intensive training, turning her capacity for murder into a highly marketable professional skill.

Like 007, Villanelle is an extremely adaptable and capable spy. Where they differ is that she has an alarming absence of remorse. Bond’s conscience often causes him to hesitate before making a kill in the novels, or even change his mind and lower his weapon. This isn’t the case for Villanelle. What’s more, she appears to take a disturbing pleasure in this aspect of her work. Bond is only ever violent when he feels it’s necessary to complete his mission. Villanelle seems to go out of her way to find solutions that require bloodshed. This means that, naturally, she ends up killing much more frequently than James Bond ever has, and ensures she’s one of the most dangerous spies ever to appear in a thriller novel.

You can purchase Codename Villanelle, the first Killing Eve novel by Luke Jennings, here.

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. Despite fresh competition during the crowded Thanksgiving period, The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping is poised to top the domestic box-office leaderboard for the second weekend in a row. The movie opened with $120 million domestically last weekend and is projected to gross around $85 million over the five-day Thanksgiving period. Two international titles — Japan's Godzilla Minus Zero and India's Ramayana: Rise of a Legend — are also expected to break major records.

Incidentally, both films opened on the same day and have delivered impressive results so far at the domestic box office. Produced on a reported budget of less than $20 million, Godzilla Minus Zero has passed the $50 million mark domestically and the $100 million mark worldwide. This weekend, it's expected to overtake its predecessor, Godzilla Minus One. Meanwhile, Ramayana is set to become the highest-grossing Indian film of all time at the domestic box office.

Sony distributed the film, and its publicity campaign began at San Diego Comic-Conearlier this year. While the film was always expected to generate interest in India and among the South Asian diaspora worldwide — it is inspired by the sacred Hindu text Ramayana — its success in North America is worth celebrating.

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

Directed by Nitesh Tiwari, the movie is also notable as the most expensive Indian film ever. Producer Namit Malhotra previously revealed the budget for both Ramayana and its planned sequel, which is expected to debut in 2028, to be more than $400 million combined. The first film follows Lord Rama on a 14-year banishment from his home kingdom, as he is accompanied byhis wife Sita and his brother Lakshman. During the banishment, Sita is abducted by the demon king of Lanka, Ravana.

The showdown, which represents the victory of good over evil and is celebrated by Hindus as Diwali, will be depicted in the sequel. Ramayana, starring Ranbir Kapoor as Lord Rama and Yash as Ravana, has grossed nearly $190 million worldwide so far. Of this total, nearly $30 million has come from North America, putting Ramayana ahead of this year's Dhurandhar: The Revenge, Kalki 2898 AD, and the 2017 blockbuster Baahubali 2: The Conclusion.

Ramayana opened in more than 1,400 theaters domestically and is now playingin just over 400 locationsas it enters its fourth week of release. The movie holds a 78% critics' score and a "Verified Hot" 93% audience score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

Rams shut down Packers in second half for a 26-13 win in NFL's first Thanksgiving Eve game

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Rams and Packers played to a tie in the first 30 minutes of Wednesday night’s game in Los Angeles, but the second half was all Rams.

Blake Corum ran for 151 yards and a score in final 30 minutes and the Rams defense shut out the Packers to pave the way to a 26-13 win. The victory is the fifth in a row for the Rams and they are now 7-4 with a Thursday night home game against the Chiefs on deck in Week 13.

The win moves the Rams ahead of the Packers in the race for NFC playoff spots and Thursday’s game between the Lions and Bears will have further impact on the chances for all four teams to advance to the postseason.

Corum’s touchdown came at the end of the Rams’ first possession of the second half and he finished the game with a career-high 32 carries for 184 yards.

That scoring drive ate up more than eight minutes. The Packers had a quick three-and-out, but briefly showed some life when linebacker Ty’Ron Hopper picked off Matthew Stafford to set the offense up in Los Angeles territory. Jordan Love turned the ball right back over when he hung a deep pass that safety Jaylen McCullough snagged out of the air. The Rams would then ride Corum and Kyren Williams on a pair of drives that covered more than 11 minutes.

The Packers’ last chance came with 6:13 left and they moved the ball to midfield before Love threw his second interception of the game. Cornerback Emmanuel Forbes got that one and the Packers were out of timeouts well before the two-minute warning.

Sticking with the ground game helped to limit Packers defensive end Micah Parsons’s impact in his return from last season’s torn ACL. He had a sack late in the first half, but Stafford only threw eight times after the break and the Packers’ run defense could not get their offense the ball often enough to get back into the game.

Love was 17-of-29 for 198 yards and a touchdown, but clearly missed wide receiver Christian Watson after he left the game with a concussion. They only picked up two first downs after halftime and they’ll hope that an extended break before their Week 13 trip to New Orleans is enough time for him to return to action.

The Rams have several injuries of their own to monitor as guard Bill Murray joined tight ends Davis Allen and Tyler Higbee in making early exits from the game. They’d like to have all those players back next week, but they showed on Wednesday night that Corum can sometimes be enough all by himself.

All 32 Episodes Of Netflix’s Gangster Thriller Masterpiece Are Perfect From Start To Finish

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

All 32 episodes of Netflix's gangster thriller masterpiece deliver gripping crime drama, unforgettable characters, and relentless suspense from start to finish. As far as great crime thrillers go on Netflix, this critically acclaimed series takes the cake as one of the greatest that the streamer has ever produced, with a truly talented ensemble cast and top-tier execution across the board from the page to the screen. If you enjoy gritty crime dramas packed with action and tension like Power and Mayor of Kingstown, consider this a must-watch on Netflix.

Top Boy, created by MobLand creator Ronan Bennett, is one of the best gangster series Netflix has ever delivered and can be called a masterpiece throughout its 32-episode run. It earned a near-perfect 97% Rotten Tomatoes score across its five seasons and is the ideal show to binge now that MobLand season 2 has wrapped up. Led by Ashley Walters of Adolescence acclaim, Top Boy is incredibly easy to get into thanks to an immersive story world that maintains a dark yet realistic tone, making it one of the most rewarding binges on Netflix.

Not only does Top Boy offer viewers thrilling gangster-fueled drama, but its lead characters, Dushane (Waters) and Sully (Kane "Kano" Robinson), are the backbone of the series with fierce ambition and fascinating moral complexity. Dushane and Sully are partners who constantly challenge each other while also working together to maintain dominance in the streets. They must deal with rival gangs while adhering to unspoken laws of the land, all while keeping their delicate power dynamic afloat. While Dushane is often calculated and highly strategic, Sully is impulsive and more prone to violence, which creates a compelling contrast between the two throughout the series.

Somewhat similar to MobLand, Top Boy is praised for its realistic depiction of underground crime in East London, touching on everything from drug dealing and turf wars to gang violence and poverty-stricken communities. The series does far more than just paint an authentic picture of this subculture, but also instills sharp social commentary and examines several systems of oppression that heavily influence the street politics and motivations within the community, known as Summerhouse. What's more, the series gives every key character intense stakes, both personally and socially, which drives the narrative engine throughout the 32-episode series, making it wildly entertaining.

Top Boy cuts through any unnecessary elements to narrowly focus on three key themes within the Summerhouse hosting estate, where Dushane and Sully live and work from: power, loyalty, and betrayal. Like most gangster or mob-related crime dramas, loyalty, or the lack thereof, becomes a central concept to the narrative framework and is the most volatile factor in determining shifts of power. Within the lawless parameters of Summerhouse, loyalty is tested on a day-to-day basis, as new ambitions arise and are acted upon, forcing head-to-head conflict and oftentimes violent outcomes. Without these themes, Top Boy would be missing the heart and soul of the show's identity.

Netflix's acclaimed 8-part true crime thriller follows two detectives pursuing a serial attacker while a young survivor struggles to be believed.

Top Boy is widely considered a masterpiece by audiences and critics alike because of the depth of its story, the authenticity of its world, and the richness of its characters. The first two seasons, originally released by Channel 4, were so impactful that Netflix acquired the rights to produce three additional seasons, the last of which won the 2024 BAFTA TV Award for Best Drama Series.

Top Boy season 5 earned a perfect 100% Rotten Tomatoes score, with The Guardian noting in its 5/5-star review, "In these last chaotic, ultra-violent episodes, Dushane and Sully are pushed to the brink – and fans get a hugely rewarding conclusion." Ultimately, if you're looking for a rewarding crime thriller masterpiece to dive into that's perfect from start to finish on Netflix, Top Boy can't be beat.

The holiday season is here. And I’m in the mood to give something away. (It must be the whiskey.)

The On Our Way Home ebook is available for free on Thursday and Friday.

Why not give something away? This is the week when everyone is trying to sell you something. No one is giving you anything, with zero strings attached.

On Our Way Home is primarily a Christmas story. It’s also a ghost story. I cranked it out between Thanksgiving and New Year’s Day a few years back.

In the interests of avoiding any spoilers, I’ll just say this: Download it and give it a try. It’s essentially about family and redemption and reconciliation. It’s also about forgiving others, while finding a way to forgive yourself.

It’s funny at times. It’s sad at times. If you make it to the end, you’ll possibly shed a tear or two.

The best feedback I’ve gotten came from a reader who had suffered from PTSD for years. Reading On Our Way Home unlocked his ability to feel emotions again.

I never intended anything like that. I just had an idea for a story, years before writing it. One year, I got caught up in the spirit of the season and it flowed, one chapter, one page, one word at a time.

The price is right. If you don’t like it, delete it. If you like it, keep going.

Speaking of free, Quarterback of the Future is being posted, one chapter at a time. Twenty-five chapters are up. A new one is coming every day until it’s done.

Someone asked recently why my break from writing consists of more writing. There’s something magical and, frankly, fun about creating something out of nothing. It’s even more fun when people read it and enjoy it.

Whether you enjoy it is up to you. If you don’t, that’s on me. But it won’t cost you a penny to find out, if you download On Our Way Home on Thursday or Friday.

Former Disney Boss Bob Iger Got Employee Pregnant During Extramarital Affair, New Book Claims

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 'The House of Mouse,' author Robbie Whelan also claims Iger was frustrated with his compensation.

Bob Iger‘s compensation frustrations and an extramarital affair with an ABC employee that resulted in a pregnancy are some of the revelations laid out in an upcoming tell-all book.

The House of Mouse, written by longtime Wall Street Journal reporter Robbie Whelan, is an unauthorized biography and corporate history of the former Disney CEO that releases Dec. 1. Iger reportedly declined to be interviewed for the book.

In the book, Whelan claimed Iger had an extramarital affair with an ABC marketer in the early 1990s while he was running ABC Entertainment at the time, according to The New York Times, which obtained an advance copy. The relationship was consensual. (Iger was previously married to Susan Iger from 1977 to 1994 and married his current wife, Willow Bay, in 1995.) Related Stories Movies Francis Lawrence Signed On for One 'Hunger Games' Movie. Five Films Later, He's the Keeper of Panem Beyond the Book 'Percy Jackson and the Olympians': How Season 3 Brings Epic Moments to Life and Deals With Annabeth's Absence in the Books

The woman, who wasn’t identified, became pregnant and “suffered through a traumatic termination procedure,” Mr. Whelan wrote. The Times noted that Whelan contrasts Iger’s personal relationship with his public support for the #MeToo movement in the late 2010s.

Elsewhere in the book, it also claimed the former Disney boss was irritated and vindictive when it came to money. In 2025, Iger received a total compensation package of $45.8 million from the company, an increase from his $41.1 million in 2024, The Hollywood Reporter previously reported.

“I am frustrated about compensation! I had a nasty and distasteful negotiation to extend my contract this last time,” Iger allegedly wrote in a private email in 2014 that was sent to Ike Perlmutter, then Marvel’s chairman and a major Disney shareholder, that Whelan obtained. In 2014, Iger earned $46.5 million, a 36 percent increase from the year prior.

Iger served as Disney CEO twice, first from 2005 to 2020 before he returned in 2022 when Bob Chapek was ousted. Josh D’Amaro took over in March of this year after Iger stepped down.

In The House of Mouse, Iger wasn’t the only Disney exec facing scathing accusations during their time running the company. Whelan also accused Chapek of mocking fans at a Marvel movie premiere in 2022.

The author recounted Chapek gesturing toward fans cosplaying as superheroes and asked, “Did you get a look at the freaks?”

THR reached out to Disney and Iger for comment. The Times reported that Chapek did not respond to their request for comment.

With Grand Theft Auto 6, The Series Has Outgrown Some Of Its Joke Brand Names

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

One thing that keeps striking me about Grand Theft Auto 6 is how good the writing is, even compared to that in previous games in the series. I enjoyed those earlier games, sure, but the dialogue and storytelling in GTA 6 are a cut above the rest in terms of both the game’s compelling characters and its worldbuilding. The fact that GTAÂ is set in an analogue of the real world helps the worldbuilding bridge gaps that most fictional universes couldnât, but Iâm increasingly finding that the standout writing is fighting a war within the game itself against some of the other jokes that have been building up in the series for over a quarter century.

Grand Theft Auto, for obvious reasons, canât use the existing brand names that we see every day in real life. In this sense it’s the opposite of the Like a Dragon series, which proudly wears all its licensed brand deals on its chest and even had to write a narrative around losing the license to a key landmark in the seventh game in the series. Talking Budweiser into lending their brand name to GTA 4 would have been an uphill battle and maybe also raised some uncomfortable questions about the gameâs proximity to real-world places and events. Better to just call your in-game equivalent Pisswasser and move on.

But weâre in 2026 now. The writing in theÂ GTA games has elevated and, to Rockstarâs credit, so have the fake brand names in general. Taco Bomb is a pretty good one as a stand-in for Taco Bell. ZIP for GAP is also good. Weazel News for Fox News makes sense on a couple of different levels, Snapmatic for Instagram is something that you recognize immediately upon hearing it. But there are a handful of brand names I think the series needs to ditch, as theyâre taking away from the more interesting narratives Rockstar wants to tell.

Pisswasser is not a bad joke on its own, but it wears its welcome out fast. Marbles, the McDonaldâs stand-in in GTA, sells a burger called Big Balls. I get it, Big Mac, Big Balls, sure. But come on, man. Weâre scraping the bottom of the barrel here. Zombix isnât even a good pun. Itâs like someone filed that one in the ten minutes before the deadline. We need to start thinking intelligently about which of these benefit worldbuilding and which detract from it.

I’m not saying all of these need to go or even a majority of them. There are plenty of good joke brand names, but there are also ones that are very, very 2000s, and it would be better for the games if they disappear. Grand Theft Auto has grown with its audience, and it’s now delivering narratives that genuinely keep up with well-written modern movies, so why are we limiting aspects of it to jokes that would appear in an Epic Movie parody?

Rockstar can and should start taking a look at these and see what makes sense for their games every so often. Audit the world, donât just assume that because it existed in previous games, it needs to exist forever.

Itâs not like brands donât just disappear from the real world all the time. Joannâs Fabrics isnât around anymore, but your world keeps spinning, just like it would if the Obey car line simply did not appear in a new game. You planning to take a Spirit Airlines flight this holiday? Youâre sure as hell not, nor would you be a sobbing mess on the floor if a Big Balls sandwich billboard wasnât overlooking a highway chase.

There are already so many good jokes in these games that the lame ones can get cut out without losing anything. No one is wrong for liking them, but maybe the games themselves can be better for examining what works and what doesnât. No series has ever escaped a need for refreshing itself and iterating over the years and, in almost every way, GTA 6 nails it. If they decide it would be better to snip, that would be fine, too.

The 10 Best Fantasy Books in Literary History, Ranked

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fantasy stories let audiences escape reality and journey to far-off lands and brand-new worlds. We’ve seen and felt these tangible worlds on screen, but many of the very best fantasy narratives began on the page. Though countless incredible fantasy books exist in literary history, these ten are simply the best.

For this list, we will include singular novels and larger book series because, well, together they complete a monumental tale. From Middle-earth to Westeros, Hogwarts to Narnia, these fantasy stories have played a critical part in our lives and pop culture at large. What are you favorite fantasy books?

For her debut novel, Susanna Clarke made a miraculous impression. Jonathan Strange & Mr. Norrell, set in an alternate 19th-century England during the Napoleonic Wars, follows the reclusive scholar Mr. Norrell and his daring pupil Jonathan Strange as they revive practical magic. As their rivalry and different approaches to magic grow, their dealings with dangerous faeries threaten to overwhelm the nation's war efforts. Jonathan Strange & Mr. Norrell is praised as a great in the genre for its blend of 19th-century literary style with dark, grounded magic. Winner of the 2005 Hugo Award for Best Novel and longlisted for the 2004 Booker Prize, Jonathan Strange & Mr. Norrell is a readable wonder.

In a rich pastiche of Jane Austen and Charles Dickens, the book reads like a lost Regency classic rather than standard modern fantasy. By playing in that realm, Clarke builds a deep fictional academic history of English magic and the Raven King, making the world feel lived-in and authentic. Her characters are brilliant and vibrant. The cautious, book-hoarding Mr. Norrell and the daring, charismatic Jonathan Strange create a compelling mentor-student rivalry through their contrasting magical views. Magic here is wild, dangerous, and tied to ancient faerie pacts rather than neat spell systems. A perfectly adaptable story, Jonathan Strange & Mr. Norrell became a BBC miniseries in 2015 starring Bertie Carvel as Jonathan Strange and Eddie Marsan as Gilbert Norrell.

One of the most beloved book series of all time is His Dark Materials by Philip Pullman. Comprising The Golden Compass, The Subtle Knife, and The Amber Spyglass, His Dark Materials follows Lyra Belacqua and Will Parry as they travel across parallel universes filled with witches, armored polar bears, and a mysterious particle called Dust. Blending coming-of-age adventure with ideas from physics, philosophy, and theology, the story challenges tyrannical religious authority while reimagining themes from John Milton's Paradise Lost. His Dark Material blends fast-paced adventure with deep literary, philosophical, and scientific ideas.

Though it’s geared for a younger audience, His Dark Materials presents adult themes without ever talking down. Pullman weaves ideas from theology, philosophy, and physics into a story led by children. Rather than treating growing up or questioning authority as a fall from grace, the trilogy treats consciousness, free will, and human connection as vital virtues. It’s a bold moral perspective. Pullman’s greatest achievement is his memorable works building. Every human in Lyra’s world has a "dæmon"—an external animal companion that reflects their inner self, shifting shapes in childhood and settling into a permanent form at adulthood. Alongside armored polar bears, witches, and parallel worlds, this idea gives emotional weight to the story's central conflicts. The novels have won several awards, including the Carnegie Medal in 1995 for Northern Lights and the 2001 Whitbread Book of the Year for The Amber Spyglass. Beyond the page, the stories have been transformed for radio plays, theater productions, and a prominent film, The Golden Compass, starring Daniel Craig and Nicole Kidman, as well as an epic three-part series featuring Dafne Keen, Ruth Wilson, James McAvoy, and Lin-Manuel Miranda.

He may be the King of Horror, but Stephen King sure knows how to write masterpieces in other genres! Such is the case for The Dark Tower. In his genre-blending epic, King tells the story of Roland Deschain, the last surviving gunslinger in a decaying, post-apocalyptic landscape known as Mid-World. Alongside a band of companions drawn from alternate realities, Roland journeys across multiple dimensions to reach and protect the titular Dark Tower, the central nexus holding all of existence together, before evil forces can destroy it and collapse the multiverse. With many fans pointing to the novels serving as the author’s magnum opus, The Dark Tower’s ambitious world-building, evolving characters, and bold meta-storytelling set it apart from all the rest.

Comprising nine books released over a span of 30 years, The Dark Tower mixes dark fantasy, Westerns, science fiction, horror, and Arthurian myth rather than sticking to traditional sword-and-sorcery tropes. Each installment takes on a distinct structure and mood—from stark wasteland survival to flashbacks and meta-fiction—which keeps the long arc unpredictable. The Dark Tower serves as the linchpin connecting many of King's other stories across shifting worlds and realities. The novel series has a passionate fandom that was none too pleased with the execution of the 2017 film starring Idris Elba and Matthew McConaughey. Fingers crossed that Mike Flanagan does it justice as a TV series!

An exemplary high-fantasy series, The Wheel of Time spans 14 books and has two credited authors: Robert Jordan and Brandon Sanderson. The epic fantasy series in a cyclical world where a prophesied hero, the Dragon Reborn, must unite nations and master dangerous magic to fight the Dark One in the Last Battle. Begun by Jordan and finished posthumously by Sanderson using Jordan's notes, the story follows young companions swept into ancient prophecies, political upheaval, and a race against madness and darkness. Praised for its massive scale, intricate world building, unique magic system, and long-term character arcs, The Wheel of Time delivers an expansive fantasy journey that has captured the imaginations of its devoted readers.

With distinct magic and themes, “channeling” is divided by gender, with male channelers facing madness from a taint on their half of the power. The story blends classic good-versus-evil stakes with cyclical time and reincarnation. Rather than infallible heroes, characters make costly mistakes, face moral ambiguity, and grow. Spanning over 4.4 million words, the series maps out diverse nations, political factions, histories, and prophecies in an intricate, distinct manner. The depth with which the novels are written proves the true dedication to storytelling. The Wheel of Time has found itself adapted into various forms of media, including the brilliant three-season wonder starring Rosamund Pike. The Wheel of Time reshaped modern epic fantasy forever, bridging fantasy past and present. ​​​​​​​

Perhaps the underdog among these titans of literature, modern fantasy wouldn’t be what it is today without A Wizard of Earthsea by Ursula K. Le Guin. The 1968 novel set in the fictional archipelago of Earthsea, a proud young wizard named Ged accidentally unleashes a dangerous shadow creature after trying to prove his power. To restore balance and save himself, he must journey across the world to confront what he has released and learn true self-mastery. A Wizard of Earthsea is praised for its lyrical prose, thoughtful coming-of-age arc, and a magic system rooted in language and balance. Part of the larger The Earthsea Cycle, the novel recalibrated the genre by shifting the genre away from epic battles and good-versus-evil clichés toward inner growth, ecological and cosmic balance, and a non-white protagonist.

Rather than focus on bloated lore, it centers on a gifted, prideful young mage who releases a shadow born of his own arrogance and must learn humility, balance, and self-mastery. Ged’s main adversary is internal, making his growth feel three-dimensional and earned. Magic is rooted in language and balance, working through true names and the language of dragons, where every spell affects the world's equilibrium and carries real consequences. Le Guin writes with a sparse, fairy-tale clarity that reads like an ancient legend. Coming long before many of the greats on this list, A Wizard of Earthsea helped establish the magic-school trope while offering a thoughtful, post-Tolkien alternative focused on wisdom over conquest. The book has been adapted multiple times in various media, namely a Sci-Fi series starring Shawn Ashmore, Kristen Kreuk, and Danny Glover, as well as a Studio Ghibli animated epic called Tales from Earthsea. ​​​​​​​

Pop culture forever changed when Harry Potter entered the literary lexicon. In the seven-book series, orphaned young wizard Harry Potter discovers his magical heritage and attends Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry, where he befriends Ron Weasley and Hermione Granger. Across seven novels, they battle the dark wizard Lord Voldemort to stop him from controlling the wizarding world. Hailed by many as the greatest fantasy series of all time, regardless of controversy, Harry Potter blends a cozy boarding-school fantasy where magic dominates the world with mystery plots and a coming-of-age arc that matures alongside its characters from ages 11 to 17. The novels brilliantly invite readers into an immersive world that weaves clues and twists into a school-year structure, keeping readers guessing and rewarding close attention.

Sparking a global phenomenon, Harry Potter is something special. The author builds a vivid magical society complete with Hogwarts, Quidditch, enchanted objects, and distinct systems of government and education that feel lived-in and timeless. The book’s ability to make the heroes feel like your friends is a testament to its rich character development. Beyond the primary trio, the books present a plethora of memorable characters across the spectrum of good and evil, even reminding readers that there is no binary when it comes to morality. Harry Potter revived youth reading worldwide, reshaped young adult fantasy publishing, and launched a massive cross-media entertainment franchise. The books were adapted into a monumental film franchise starring Daniel Radcliffe. The stories are being revived for the small screen on HBO with a brand-new ensemble, and will be more book-focused and faithful. ​​​​​​​

Let’s just state it now: this is entry number one of two for J.R.R. Tolkien. In the grand scope of fantasy, The Hobbit is the novel that set the tone for the century and beyond. As an early entry point for young readers, The Hobbit follows Bilbo Baggins, a comfort-loving hobbit recruited by the wizard Gandalf and thirteen dwarves to serve as their burglar on a quest to reclaim their ancestral Lonely Mountain and its treasure from the dragon Smaug. Along the perilous journey, Bilbo discovers a magical ring of invisibility and grows from a timid homebody into a courageous, resourceful hero. The Hobbit works so well because it pairs a brisk, accessible adventure with rich world-building, relatable growth, and timeless themes of courage and greed.

At around 300 pages, it reads like a lively, warm quest with a lighter, more self-contained arc compared to its sibling story. Tolkien mixes playful humor, songs, and riddles with real suspense and darker moments. Unlike sprawling epics that can bog down in exposition, The Hobbit moves in a masterful manner from set piece to set piece while letting characters drive the story. Bilbo Baggins starts out loving comfort and quiet routines rather than grand heroism, making his growth feel grounded and human. For many readers, he’s one of the greatest literary heroes ever written. With a whimsical charm and dangerous stakes woven into the narrative, The Hobbit taps into timeless archetypes of self-discovery. With a plentiful number of adaptations since its release, The Hobbit is most certainly remembered for its 1977 animated television special. And then there was that trilogy between 2012 and 2013 starring Martin Freeman, which we appreciate was made, though a redo would be nice. It’s no match for Peter Jackson’s other Tolkien trilogy!​​​​​​​

C.S. Lewis is truly a magician with words, and The Chronicles of Narnia is proof. Across seven books, The Chronicles of Narnia follows children from the real world who are magically transported to Narnia, a fantastical realm of talking animals and mythical creatures. Guided by the noble lion Aslan, these young heroes must fulfill ancient prophecies and battle evil forces to restore peace across Narnia's entire history, from its creation to its ultimate end. The novels are universally praised for combining accessible portal fantasy with rich moral themes, vivid imagery, and stories that span an entire world's history from creation to its end. Lewis delivers immediate wonder, bold storytelling, and deep moral weight without heavy exposition.

Lewis was an early leader in the genre, helping elevate fantasy and children's literature by treating big ideas, danger, and loss honestly rather than talking down to readers. Rather than rigid world-building, C.S. Lewis blends Roman fauns, talking animals, mythical creatures, and Father Christmas into a lively, fairy-tale atmosphere. Ordinary children step through a wardrobe—or into other portals—into Narnia with almost no setup or dense lore dump. And yet, these worlds become brilliantly immersive, allowing the celebration of imagination to roam free. The series shifts perspectives across installments, but Lewis maintains the narrative DNA as it expands the world throughout the timeline. Through the Christ-like lion Aslan and broader narrative arcs from creation to an end-times, Lewis communicated Christian ideas through story rather than direct lecturing. No matter your ideology, the books still resonate. The volumes of books have been adapted three times on TV and, with Netflix releasing Narnia: The Magician’s Nephew, twice for film, with the Walden Media franchise presenting a beautiful trilogy. ​​​​​​​

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

Though it existed long before the hit HBO series, A Song of Ice and Fire is high fantasy at its finest. Written by George R.R. Martin, consisting of five existing books at the moment, A Song of Ice and Fire is set in the medieval continent of Westeros, where rival noble families engage in a brutal political civil war for control of the Seven Kingdoms. As these human power struggles unfold, ancient magic slowly returns in the form of Daenerys Targaryen's reborn dragons in the east and an existential threat of undead creatures gathering beyond a massive ice wall in the far north. Praised for its morally complex characters, ruthless political intrigue, and stories where choices carry real consequences, A Song of Ice and Fire grounds the fantasy in human motives while subverting classic genre tropes.

What A Song of Ice and Fire does brilliantly is its unpredictability. No one is safe to Martin's pen. Major figures can be killed or maimed, rejecting traditional fantasy comfort for a high-tension thrill ride. The morally gray characters operate in a middle ground where their complex motives override the clear heroic or villainous traits. Martin paints Westeros as a brilliantly rich land where power struggles, alliances, and the harsh realities of survival drive the action. Shifting third-person limited viewpoints allow readers to experience deep personal perspectives across a vast, detailed world. A Song of Ice and Fire had a monumental impact that reshaped modern fantasy. Though the question remains: will it ever get finished!? Whether you've read the collection or not, Martin's vision came to life in the eight-season series Game of Thrones. There was a definitive ending, though based on fans' reactions, the final two planned novels may veer in a vastly different direction! Fingers crossed, at least!

We greatly revere J.R.R. Tolkien because he is such a prolific writer. The Lord of the Rings is all the proof you need. The epic high fantasy novel, broken up into three parts, is set in Middle-earth wher hobbits, wizards, elves, and men unite against the Dark Lord Sauron. It's central quest centers on the journey to destroy the One Ring in Mount Doom to thwart Sauron's campaign to conquer the realm. Through deep world-building, timeless themes of hope and friendship, and its foundational influence on modern fantasy, The Lord of the Rings has had a lasting cultural reach beyond its initial release.

Tolkien built Middle-earth over decades, grounding the story in deep lore, past ages, and fallen kingdoms. His vast vision became a benchmark for scale and depth for fantasy storytelling. He crafted distinct languages, scripts, and histories for peoples, making Middle-earth feel lived-in rather than a simple backdrop. Shaped by the author's experience in World War I and his views of industrialism, the story examines the corrupting pull of power, the cost of war, and the value of everyday courage and compassion. Humble hobbits carry the weight of stopping a massive evil, showing how quiet courage and persistence can change the world. Tolkien moves the narrative from cozy beginnings to high-stakes battles and tragic moments, offering readers a wide emotional range. With over 150 million copies sold, it has inspired countless media adaptations, most notably Peter Jackson's Oscar-winning trilogy. Whether it's your favorite fantasy story or not, its lasting impact on the genre, pop culture, and entertainment is undeniable.

5 Greatest Classic Fantasy Books To Read in the Winter

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

As the temperatures drop and the Christmas lights begin to twinkle, you might find yourself wanting to enjoy some type of media. Maybe it's a great classic movie — lord knows there's enough of those — or maybe it's playing Mariah Carey's signature tune on loop — more power to you, if that's the case. However, you might be looking for something a bit more long-term, and that's where books come in.

Fortunately for you, there's a fair share of great classic books to enjoy over the winter, especially in the realm of fantasy. Perhaps it's a good, old-fashioned chivalry romance set in the distant imaginary past, or maybe it's a thrilling adventure in a far-away, fantastical land. Whatever the story, these fantasy books are the ideal choice for you to enjoy over the next three months, wrapped in a blanket, with a cup of warm cocoa by your side and the will to immerse yourself in magical realms where everything is possible.

There have been several retellings and reinterpretations of the classic Arthurian legend, but none is more defining or prevalent than T.H. White's. Drawing from the equally seminal La Morte D'Arthur, The Once and Future King is comprised of four parts — The Sword in the Stone, The Queen of Air and Darkness, The Ill-Made Knight, and The Candle in the Wind — and chronicles Arthur's life, from his childhood as "Ward" to his time as Camelot's greatest ruler.

The Once and Future King offers an entire, comprehensive fantastical journey through the life of one of literature's most iconic figures. It begins as a whimsical and charming coming-of-age tale of magic and culminates in a severe and tragic story of duty and fate. Few books, fantasy or otherwise, have ever presented such a complete and rewarding depiction of the Hero's Journey. Everything you'd ever wanted to know about the Arthurian legend takes shape here, with the book featuring all the major characters. It's the definitive Arthurian tale, and a perfect book to experience throughout the chilly winter.

Peter S. Beagle's The Last Unicorn is a sad fairy tale that offers plenty of food for thought. The plot centers on the titular Unicorn, an immortal being who learns she might be the last of her kind. Thus, she abandons her enchanted forest and goes on a quest to find out what happened to her species, meeting colorful characters along the way and eventually reaching the castle of the depressed King Haggard.

The Last Unicorn might seem like an unexpected choice. However, its melancholy tone is perfect for a season where, despite all the joy, one can also feel a distinct and gloomy vibe at times. There's a real sense of loss here, with the book exploring themes of mortality and regret, two ideas that many find themselves wrestling with during the happiest season. It's not all gloom here, though, as the Unicorn learns the value of love and its importance to a well-lived life, but it is a rather wistful tale that might leave you feeling a bit blue. Yet, it's also tremendously enjoyable, a perfect combo for the winter season.

When discussing fantasy sagas, Robert Jordan's Wheel of Time ranks near the top. Producing a whopping 15 novels — 3 of which were co-authored by Brandon Sanderson after Jordan's passing in 2007 — the series is among the most complex and expansive in the genre. It all began with The Eye of the World, where a group of small-town boys go on a perilous journey after learning that one of them is the Dragon Reborn, destined to either save or destroy the world.

The Eye of the World might just be the perfect Fall book. It begins with a warm, almost pastoral approach as it introduces the main characters and their little town in the region of Two Rivers, before switching to a colder, darker vibe once they leave the safety of what they know to begin their journey. It's not exactly grimdark, though, so while the plot does get more somber, it never becomes actively tough to endure. Instead, The Eye of the World is a clever subversion of the Hero's Journey, featuring a cast of relatable characters and a world that is as inviting as it's dangerous and changing.

Now on to a book that is pure winter wonder. Philip Pullman's Northern Lights is set in a world where people's souls are physically manifested as animals. The main plot centers on Lyra Belacqua, a twelve-year-old who becomes entangled in the mysterious disappearances of children, including her friend, Roger. Eventually, her search leads her to the Arctic, where she'll face polar bears, befriend nomadic people, and learn about the mysterious substance known as Dust.

Out of all the books on this list, Northern Lights is the one that might seem the most obvious to enjoy during the winter. Everything about it, from its initial English setting to the Arctic scenes in the later half, screams winter — heck, the title alone is enough to get you in a chilly mood! The entire saga might be aimed at teen audiences, but it's actually rather deep, dealing with themes of oppression and religious hypocrisy, a fact that has made it quite controversial. Even so, His Dark Materials is a must-read for every fantasy fan, and there's no better time to experience it than in winter.

What better way to end than with a show where the very idea of winter is so central that it's practically a plot point in itself? "Winter is coming," George R.R. Martin told us in A Game of Thrones, a dark fantasy saga set in the hyper-violent and ruthless lands of Westeros and Essos. The novel follows honorable Ned Stark as he becomes Hand of the King to his drunken friend, King Robert Baratheon, placing him at the center of a dangerous murder mystery that will lead to his tragic end. in the North, a threat rises from beyond the Wall, while across the Narrow Sea, exiled princess Daenerys Targaryen marries a Dothraki warlord.

A Game of Thrones is one of the most celebrated fantasy novels of the last 30 years. Martin created one of the most vivid and complex worlds in fantasy, a grimdark masterpiece that would completely revolutionize the genre in the new millennium. The plot is set at the end of a long summer, as the threat of winter looms in the distance. Blending intense, brutal action with a more intimate mystery set in the cutthroat court of King's Landing, A Game of Thrones is a masterclass in high fantasy writing, kickstarting a franchise that eventually spawned arguably the most defining TV shows of the 2010s.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.
