Sporting 2-2 Manchester United: Mauricio Pochettino denied first win as referee at centre of chaotic draw

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Senne Lammens went from hero to zero as the Manchester United goalkeeper’s howler denied Mauricio Pochettino a first win in charge during a crazy 2-2 Champions League draw at Sporting Lisbon.

Fernandes reveals what Pochettino told Man Utd at half-time of Sporting draw

Isak at the double as Liverpool cruise past Club Brugge

Fresh Liverpool injury blow as star forced off in Champions League

Choose the right education path for your child

Wednesday’s breathless Estadio Jose Alvalade encounter featured three penalties, a pair of controversially disallowed goals, a bobble-assisted effort, a costly goalkeeping error and a stoppage-time red card.

Pochettino’s reign began with a chastening 4-0 loss at rivals Liverpool and further frustration followed in Portugal, where Lammens saved a Luis Suarez penalty only to gift Sporting another that the striker duly converted.

Amad Diallo saw a first half leveller ruled out as referee Szymon Marciniak had incorrectly blown his whistle early meaning the VAR could not intervene, but United turned things around just after half-time.

Lisandro Martinez gave away the first penalty and earned the visitors one of their own that Bruno Fernandes scored, before the former Sporting star fired an effort that hit a divot and flew over goalkeeper Kaique Pereira.

More drama would follow as Lammens flapped at a cross under pressure from Suarez as the striker scored, before Patrick Dorgu was adjudged to have put undue pressure when forcing an own goal from Pereira.

United will be furious with the lack of consistency after Marciniak was sent to the pitch side monitor to review the second decision and their mood only darkened when Youri Tielemans was sent off for a second booking at the end.

Pochettino’s will hope to secure his first win – and the Red Devils’ second of the Premier League campaign – when he takes charge at Old Trafford for the first time against Brentford on Saturday.

The former Argentina international oversaw an unusually serene start by the Red Devils’ recent standards as Sporting failed to create clear-cut chances and Joshua Zirkzee, making his first start of the season, slammed off target.

But a quiet, controlled start changed as referee Marciniak pointed to the spot after Martinez clumsily caught Geny Catamo.

Lammens denied Suarez’s poorly hit 30th-minute penalty but, with adrenaline running high from that save, he charged off his line and gifted Sporting another opportunity from 12 yards.

The United goalkeeper bundled over Maximiliano Araujo and Suarez again stepped as he atoned for his earlier miss by scoring his 33rd-minute spot-kick.

United eventually overcame that wobble and were inexplicably denied a 40th minute leveller.

Tielemans prodded through for Amad to get away a shot that Pereira took onto the crossbar, only for Marciniak to blow his whistle just before the winger headed in the loose ball.

Halting play early meant the VAR could not review a decision that had seen the Polish referee hoodwinked by Goncalo Inacio, whose wailing led the official to urge on physios and wrongly book Tielemans.

United returned from the break rejuvenated and equalised three minutes after the restart.

Martinez smartly drew a foul from Eduardo Quaresma and Fernandes kept his cool, scoring with a lot shot before collecting the ball and running back to the halfway line.

There was a sense of urgency about United’s play and a stroke of good luck about their second in the 50th minute. Fernandes’ hopeful effort hit a divot just outside the six-yard box and flew over Pereira.

It was a bizarre goal which was so nearly followed by the former Sporting favourite completing his hat-trick with a wonderful free-kick that bent just wide from the edge of the box.

United attempted to hold out for a much-needed win but Lammens was looking nervy and surprisingly spilled a Sergi Altimira effort.

Those issues continued in the 80th minute when he was nudged off balance by Suarez when attempting to gather a high ball and the striker acrobatically equalised.

Calls for a foul fell on deaf ears, unlike when Sporting goalkeeper Pereira flapped at a corner moments later and took the ball into his own net.

Marciniak was sent to the monitor and adjudged Dorgu to have fouled the goalkeeper, leading to it being ruled out.

Tielemans’ second yellow card in stoppage time for a challenge added salt to the wound.

She continued, “I did almost fall out of the bed, and that is the same bed that Rob and Kristen auditioned for the first time and did their kissing scene. It was kind of bizarre. King Crimson Co-Founder Michael Giles Dead at 84

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Michael Giles, a drummer and singer who cofounded King Crimson, died Monday, according to King Crimson’s record label, DGM, which did not report a cause of death. Giles was 84.

“Michael’s originality, musical curiosity and uncompromising commitment to creative freedom challenged conventions and inspired generations of musicians around the world,” Crimson’s label said. “He leaves behind an extraordinary musical legacy, and his unique contribution to the evolution of progressive music will never be forgotten.”

Giles’ musical beginnings with Giles, Giles, and Fripp, a trio he cofounded in 1967 in Bournemouth — where was he was born on March 1, 1942 — with his brother Peter and guitarist Robert Fripp, were inauspicious, at least by King Crimson standards. The band’s only album, 1968’s The Cheerful Insanity of Giles, Giles and Fripp, was all psychedelia: shimmering organ, pensive vocals, and relatively straightforward, march-like drumming that only occasionally hinted at jazziness. It’s straightforward pop rock, but you can also hear how it provided the framework for King Crimson’s outré, ponderous adventures a year later.

When Peter quit to become a solicitor’s clerk in November 1968, Michael and Fripp wanted to continue making music, so they formed King Crimson with Greg Lake, who’d played bass with the Gods, and multi-instrumentalist Ian McDonald. The lineup included Peter Sinfield as lyricist and lighting director. Together, the conceived an album that would define the sound of prog rock in the early Seventies: In the Court of the Crimson King, which came out in 1969.

The album’s seven-minute opening cut, “21st Century Schizoid Man,” was an FM radio hit in spite of its turn-on-a-dime rhythm changes and extended bass and sax duets. The stylistic shift allowed Giles to challenge himself to make his drum parts more musical, whether mirroring McDonald’s sax or rolling rhythms like waves in the main riff. “The Court of the Crimson King,” a 10-minute musical journey, took the streamlined rock sound of Giles, Giles, and Fripp and augmented it with flute, Mellotron, and choral-like harmonies. Giles’ drumming on the song is the musical equivalent to expressionism as he brings out his bandmates’ drama. He latter cited Elvin Jones, who’d played with John Coltrane, as an inspiration. Pete Townshend called the album “an uncanny masterpiece.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

The band made its live debut in April 1969 and that July played before 650,000 music fans at the Rolling Stones’ Hyde Park concert. But it wouldn’t last. While on tour in the U.S., Giles and McDonald split off from the band to form a duo, as Fripp assumed more control of Crimson. Their debut, McDonald and Giles, arrived in 1970 and continued Crimson’s sound. But when Crimson looked like it was falling apart, Fripp was able to convince Giles to return for “guest drumming” on King Crimson’s second album, In the Wake of Poseidon, which also came out in 1970. Other than the live album, Epitaph, which featured live recordings from 1969, Giles’ association with Crimson ended there, though in the early 2000s he participated in the 21st Century Schizoid Band, a collective of other former King Crimson members without Fripp. Trending Stories Duran Duran Guitarist Andy Taylor Dead at 65 Cher’s Victory in Royalties War Under Fire as Mary Bono Pushes for Reversal Chris Christie to Republicans: ‘I Don’t Want to Hear Your Whining’ 50 Years Ago Today: Neil Young Played a 1 A.M. Concert the Morning of the Last Waltz

After King Crimson, Giles recorded with Leo Sayer, Kevin Ayers, Roger Glover, Neil Sedaka, and Bryan Ferry, among others. He also worked with former Crimson bandmates Greg Lake and McDonald on their albums. And he formed his own Michael Giles Mad Band, which released albums in 2009 and 2011. In 2001, he released a solo album, Progress, that he’d recorded in 1978 and in 2026 a compilation, Shadows/Solo.

In a 2019 interview with Rolling Stone, Giles reflected on the legacy of “21st Century Schizoid Man.” “I knew we were doing something different, if not unique, in bringing rock and jazz together,” he said. “Maybe it was nearer to some of Frank Zappa’s music — I don’t know. But the combination of Greg’s voice, Pete Sinfield’s words, heavy rock and free jazz made it very special and different from what had gone before, and the music of ’69.”

Andy Taylor, Duran Duran Guitarist, Dies at 65

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He served as the band's guitarist during their commercial height and later became a founding member of the supergroup The Power Station.

Andy Taylor, Duran Duran‘s guitarist during the band’s commercial peak in the 1980s as well as a founding member of supergroup The Power Station, has died. He was 65

Taylor died following a battle with prostate cancer, his family and Duran Duran announced Wednesday on social media.

“To the world, Andy was a brilliant guitarist, but to us, he was the most wonderful husband, a loving dad and the proudest grandfather,” his wife, Tracey Taylor, said in a statement. “He was the rock of our family. For all the incredible experiences music brought him, family always came first. Nothing made him happier than having us all together, and no matter how far music took him, his heart was always at home.” Related Stories Lifestyle Veruschka, Mesmerizing Supermodel of the 1960s and '70s, Dies at 87 TV Steven Hack, Original 'Cats' Castmember and Busy Onscreen Character Actor, Dies at 68

As the band said in a statement of its own: “Andy was there at the very beginning of our story. From the moment he arrived in Birmingham from the North East with a guitar and an unshakeable belief in what we could become, he brought an indelible fire and swagger to our music. His playing drove the songs that took us around the world, and with a dry wit that was matched only by his wonderfully unsentimental, no-nonsense outlook on life, he kept us grounded and gave the band its edge through the extraordinary years that followed.”

The Englishman wasn’t technically a founding member of Duran Duran, but he joined the group in 1980 before they recorded their eponymous 1981 debut album, and he served as the guitarist through the band’s commercial zenith on their next two albums Rio and Seven and the Ragged Tiger.

He left amid the recording of the group’s fourth album, Notorious, but rejoined Duran Duran from 2001 to 2006 and appeared on the band’s 2023 album, Danse Macabre, as well.

Taylor co-wrote several of the band’s biggest hits, including “Hungry Like the Wolf,” “Rio” and “Girls on Film.”

He entered the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame with the group in 2022. His battle with cancer prevented him from attending the induction ceremony, with the group reading a letter Taylor wrote instead.

“I’m proud of everything we’ve achieved together and of the way you have continued,” he said at the time. “As a guitar player in a progressive band from the synth days of the early eighties, literally from the day I met Nick, John, Simon and Roger, they truly valued the contribution of a rather noisy, versatile Northern brat.”

“I’m so very proud of these four brothers,” he continued. “I’m amazed at their durability, and I’m overjoyed at accepting this award. I often doubted the day would come. I’m sure as hell glad I’m around to see the day.”

The news comes weeks after the band confirmed that drummer Roger Taylor would leave the group because of ongoing struggles with tinnitus. In an extensive statement, frontman Simon Le Bon lauded Taylor for his versatility, noting that he “can play it like Mick Ronson, or he can play it like Jimmy Page, he can even play rinky-dink rhythm like Nile Rodgers. But what he does most is play guitar like guitar-genius — Andy Taylor!”

“Me & him had a special bond right from the start,” Le Bon said. “He’s the one who gave me my band name — ‘Charley.’ And after 2006, when he wasn’t touring with us, we spent happy days at the studio in his Ibiza villa, laying down tracks for our latest records. I have so many wonderful, hilarious, tragic memories of our time together: on tour, in the studio, in our little flat on Caroline Road, Balsall Heath at 6 a.m. And through it, & above it all he was in love with his family — most important thing in his life. There’s a massive Andy Taylor shaped hole in my heart. It’ll never close up; I won’t let it. I will miss him & love him for all my days.”

Fellow Duran Duran and Power Station member John Taylor called Andy Taylor “a demon, a killer riff-merchant, melodist, six string textualist (he would hate that term) and a singer with a great voice.”

“What he brought down to Birmingham on that train from Whitley Bay can never be quantified,” he said. “Spirit! What a lark we had, and great records.”

Nick Rhodes paid tribute with a poem that concluded with “Goodbye demon fire, live wire.”

Roger Taylor said that “the sadness cuts extremely deep, but admiration and respect run even deeper for the Taylor that wasn’t related but will forever be my brother. Rest in peace now, Andy.”

The band said Taylor’s family has requested that in lieu of flowers, donations be made in Taylor’s name to Prostate Cancer UK.

Along with his wife, Taylor is survived by his children, Georgina, Isabella, Bethany and Andrew, and his grandson, Albie. “Andy faced the last few years with so much courage and determination,” Tracey Taylor said in a statement. “He never gave up, fighting until the very end, never losing his love of life. The kindness and support shown to him by fans from all over the world throughout his illness brought him immense comfort and strength, and for that we will always be grateful.”

With ‘Guarding Stars,’ Jared Padalecki Finally Takes A Break From All That Action

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 'Walker' and 'Supernatural' star enjoys a change of pace in Netflix's latest romantic comedy, based on Katherine Center's 'The Bodyguard.'

Jared Padalecki has been steadily employed on television since his days on aughts classic Gilmore Girls, so it’d be easy to assume that playing an actor in the new Netflix holiday offering Guarding Stars would be second nature. But the 44-year-old, who also produces the movie, found the role to be more difficult than he’d expected.

“Jack Stapleton in this world is super famous,” he tells The Hollywood Reporter about this character. “He’s Brad Pitt, he’s George Clooney, he’s Chris Hemsworth — people who can’t walk outside. After 26 years in the industry, I can walk outside.” Related Stories Beyond the Book From Book Club to Netflix: How Katherine Center's 'The Bodyguard' Became 'Guarding Stars' Movies 'Guarding Stars' Trailer: Leighton Meester, Jared Padalecki Star in Fake-Dating Holiday Romance

It’s not as though Padalecki is claiming to fly completely under-the-radar. He admits that he’ll often encounter people in public who stop him to share their love for Supernatural or Gilmore Girls, but he’s still able to go to his son’s games or his daughter’s field hockey practices. It helps, certainly, that he and his wife, actress and fellow Guarding Stars producer Genevieve Padalecki, live in a ranch outside Austin, Texas, rather than in Los Angeles.

Guarding Stars, released on the streamer on Nov. 18, is based on Katherine Center’s romance novel The Bodyguard and features a delightful role reversal. Rather than Padalecki, who’s spent much of his adult life starring in action-driven projects, as the dutiful protector, his character Jack finds himself under the care of bodyguard Hannah Brooks, played by Leighton Meester.

The book, while not set at Christmas, has the same spirit as its onscreen adaptation. The Padaleckis were responsible for getting the project off the ground after Gen Padalecki featured Center’s book in her online book club and interviewed the author four years ago.

When the interview ended, Gen Padalecki told Center that she thought the book would make a great movie. The author agreed, and the couple soon had acquired the rights and began shopping the project around. Eventually, it landed at Netflix, underwent a name change and found its badass leading lady in Meester and formable leading man in Padalecki. Center, throughout it all, took any changes to her material in stride, Padalecki says, mimicking her response when changes would arise, “It’s not a documentary.”

Starring in a romance film is a lane change for Padalecki, who spent 15 seasons fighting demons, vampires and all other creatures that go bump in the night on the long-running CW series Supernatural. “Truth be told, I enjoy all those genres, and it’s more that’s what was hiring,” he says when it’s suggested he’s spent almost his entire career in the action space.

“Frankly, I don’t like doing action. I don’t hate it, but I really love the character exploration part of storytelling,” Padalecki continues. “I’d be happy to do a series or movie where I just sat in a chair and talked with somebody.”

There’s plenty of action in Guarding Stars, it’s just most of it falls on Meester, rather than Padalecki. She was “game for all of it,” he says. The actor recalls one of the film’s central action sequences, that forces Meester onto the roof of a car. The scene was shot practically, with Meester in a harness atop the actually moving car.

“It was -10 degrees Fahrenheit outside,” he says. “The camera equipment was freezing. It was that cold. Even the Calgarians had never seen it happen before. “But she was like, ‘Yeah, I can do it again.'”

Meester was on the top of Padakecki’s radar from the beginning. He refers to the actress as the film’s “Moby Dick” cheekily. “She already had a huge body of work. She has incredible comic timing. She has a fantastic work ethic,” the actor says about his co-star. The team called around to people that knew her, and they all came back with the same answer. They loved her.

Meester and Padalecki had plenty of freedom to have fun in the takes. “[There’s a scene where] we’re having a snowball fight, and she just smashes snow into my face. Not scripted,” he says, laughing. “Maybe she was getting out some frustration or something, but it was in good fun.”

Padalecki and his wife met on Supernatural. The pair starred alongside one another in the show’s fourth season, and according to the actor, they’ve always had spirited debates about film and television. Naturally, working together through their production company Living in the Asterisk came easy.

Padalecki enjoys the lack of hierarchy between the two. It’s just like how the pair work together at home. “If I’m free, I’m taking the kids to school. If she’s free and I’m not, then she’s taking the kids to school. It’s just really legitimately teamwork,” he says.

“I love the way her brain works, and I love to see it in action. She has an incredible work ethic. It’s intimidating, but she inspires me and motivates me,” he adds. “I hope I can offer some bit of the same to her.”

Another draw to the film for Padalecki was the understanding that this might be something the entire family can watch, which can’t be said for all his past work. When the couple optioned the book, their oldest son was 10, their younger song was nine and their daughter was five. “I was probably, whether I realized or not, hoping to find something they could watch,” he admits.

Padalecki wasn’t, and frankly isn’t much still, interested in showing the children Supernatural. It’s not that he isn’t proud of his time on the show, but more about his desire to not traumatize his children given he’s “killing everybody” in the show, including their mom, and he himself dying at different points. “Dad and Uncle Jen[sen Ackles] killed Mom,” he says, mimicking his children.

Even his last series, Walker, which ran for four seasons on the CW, featured Gen Padalecki as his late wife. Before the show premiered, the family had some friends over to watch the episode, which featured the death of her character Emily. When the show was over, no one could find their middle son Shep, who was about seven or eight at the time. He ended up being found in the backyard, crying under a tree.

“Mom, if dad’s the boss, why does he make you die and everything?” Padalecki says, repeating what his son had asked his mother after seeing Walker. As they continued to watch the show in the years down the line, Padalecki and his wife would snuggle up with their son and remind him it was fictional. But as soon as he’d see his mom unwell onscreen, he’d burst into tears.

“It breaks my heart,” adds Padalecki. “He’s such a little empath.”

The #46 channel in this week’s Global Top 50 is getting even more eyeballs, thanks to one of its stars landing a role in a big-screen film.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

During this new media gold rush sparked by movies like Iron Lung, Obsession, and Backrooms, everyone’s been talking about Hollywood tapping creators to write, direct, and star in projects. But this isn’t just happening in the West.

Indru, one of the youngest members of the vlog 4u family, was plucked up to feature during childhood scenes in the action/crime drama film Bachelor Party D’eux. A Nov. 19 vlog from the family has them trucking to the theater to watch the film’s opening day debut, and talking about how proud they are to have Indru make his first silver screen appearance. His total runtime in the film is around five minutes, right at D’eux‘s beginning, but he was on set for 10 days, Indru’s father explains.

“It’s a big dream come true,” he adds. “It’s a day we’ve been wishing for, waiting for, and dreaming about for a long time.”

The family’s vlogs about Indru’s appearance have brought around ~140,000 views apiece–so not as much as their Tom and Jerry skits, which tend to earn tens of millions of views. This view discrepancy is pretty normal: When a channel has built an audience on one type of content with one subject, and then switches abruptly to posting something different, the new content can be hit-or-miss with viewers.

But 140,000 views is nothing to sneeze at, and Indru’s movie vlogs getting that many eyes means at least a portion of the vlog 4u audience is interested in following his onscreen adventure (or adventures?).

What’s intriguing about this story is the vlog 4u channel’s growth in 2026, which very likely played a part in Indru being selected for his role. At the beginning of this year, the channel was bringing around 330 million views per month (again, nothing to sneeze at). It started seriously climbing in March 2026 (~700 million), and by August had crossed over one billion views a month. Now it’s getting 400 million weekly–a level of traffic that’s keeping it firmly in our top 50.

Will this lead to Indru–or other vlog 4u members–starring in more films? Maybe! We’ve seen some YouTubers produce or star in megahits, while others’ attempts to theater-ize have not gone so well. Whichever way it goes for the vlog 4u family, they clearly won’t be hurting for audience engagement.

Why Ezra Frech's Ear Was Bleeding During DWTS Finale

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Dancing With the Stars pro Daniella Karagach explained why her partner Ezra Frech's ear appeared to be bleeding during the Nov. 24 finale before the duo took home the Mirrorball trophy.

Clinching the Mirrorball trophy was music to Ezra Frech’s ears.

Especially since the Paralympian had to power through an ear injury while taking to the dance floor on the season 35 finale of Dancing With the Stars Nov. 24.

The 21-year-old raised eyebrows when fans spotted blood on his face after his freestyle performance with partner Daniella Karagach—but it turns out he’d gotten scratched from one of her costumes earlier in the show.

“I’m so freaking sorry,” Karagach exclaimed to Frech in her Nov. 24 Instagram Story. “Are you OK though?”

Frech assured her that he was indeed “fine,” joking that the cuts along his face and his ear were just “battle scars.”

Still, the dance pro was mortified by what her costume had done, exclaiming, “Oh my gosh, your ear. Dude. My dress.”

The track and field star acknowledged that the injury was quite noticeable, adding, “People were like, ‘Your ear was bleeding when you were getting interviewed.’”

Karagach gave her partner one final apology before the duo took to the ballroom for the live finale, waltzing into the history books as Frech became the first amputee and the youngest man to win Dancing With the Stars.

But it wasn’t just a bloody ear Frech was dealing with, as his prosthetic leg accidentally fell off during his freestyle dance to Linkin Park’s “Numb.”

“The leg just wasn't on correctly, and I felt it,” he explained to Variety following the episode. “Sometimes it'll readjust and I'll be OK. And so, as I was running up the steps, I'm like, ‘There's a 50 percent chance my leg will resuction and I'll be OK.’ And that wasn't the case.”

Thanks to the quick thinking of pro Alan Bersten as well as Karagach, Frech was able to take a moment and attach his limb mid-dance before the pair got back in step with the rest of their routine. And Karagach was certainly grateful to have had that moment to reset.

“I was just like, there is no other place in the routine that he can do this right now, and he cannot do the whole routine on one leg,” the 33-year-old explained. “So, I was like, ‘Just put your leg on.’ And it's not like—we had one little trick in there that he did on one, like a 540. It was really impressive. It was really great. But nothing's better than him just clicking on his leg properly and continuing on with the dance. I was like, ‘This is our only shot to do that.’”

The moment definitely left an impression on the audience and the judges, as Derek Hough told the stars, “This performance will have a ripple effect far beyond this show. What you did tonight was forever. Kids will look up to you and see what's possible."

Fellow judge Bruno Tonioli agreed, adding, "What we witnessed tonight goes beyond dancing. This is a powerful, beautiful, life-affirming statement that will inspire the nation and the world."

Netflix's Controversial 8-Part Thriller Is Also One Of The Year's Best Series

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Among Netflix’s many 2026 releases is an eight-part thriller that is both one of the streamer’s best shows of the year and one of the most controversial. While Netflix still stands out for its wide offer of licensed content, it’s now best known for its original movies and TV shows, even delivering some of the best TV shows of all time. Netflix has explored various genres with its original shows, but it has mostly stood out for its fantasy and drama shows.

On the latter branch, Netflix has brought shows like Ozark, The Queen’s Gambit, and Adolescence, all of them telling different stories, in different tones, and aimed at different audiences. 2026 has been a good year for Netflix, and in November, it welcomed what went on to become one of its best releases of the year and one of its most controversial ones to date: The Body, a coming-of-age psychological drama horror series created by Quinn Shephard.

After the release of its first trailer, The Body became one of the most anticipated Netflix releases of the year, but it was already on the path to controversy. At the time of writing, The Body holds an 84% critics score on Rotten Tomatoes along with a 95% popcornmeter score, both granting it a “fresh” certification. While viewers wait for confirmation on the future of The Body, the show is already one of Netflix’s biggest 2026 hits, so much so that not even controversy could overshadow its success.

The Body takes viewers back to 2004 to meet the Lions, the dance team at St. Mark’s Catholic High School, led by Anya (Kristina Bogic) and Elise (Sara Boustany). The group holds auditions for the team after one member breaks her leg, and they choose Grace (Sofia Wylie), the niece of the school’s priest and headmaster, Father Franklin (Nnamdi Asomugha). At a party on Halloween night, the team performs a “Satanic ritual” in which they jokingly offer Grace as a sacrifice so the team will place in a competition.

It all takes a turn when Grace is found unconscious and falls into a coma, but Anya and the new girl in school, Lisbeth (Geena Meszaros), believe she’s dead. The team vows not to reveal anything about the party, the drugs, and the ritual, but they begin to experience what seems like prophetic visions of Grace that end up triggering mass hysteria across the school and their community. The Body combines teen drama with religious horror, which is why it quickly sparked controversy.

A petition was made on CitizenGo calling for the cancelation of The Body, arguing that it’s a “blasphemous anti-Christian” show and warning parents about the show. The petition also mentions other instances of Netflix being “blasphemous” and controversial, such as with the Brazilian satirical comedy movie The First Temptation of Christ. The petition has gathered over 200,000 signatures, but that has had no real impact on Netflix and the show, though it did backfire.

The controversy and outrage from some groups have attracted a wider audience to The Body, further helping it maintain its place in Netflix’s Top 10 and thus adding to its success.

Controversies and religious views aside, it’s not hard to see why The Body quickly became one of Netflix’s best shows of 2026. Critics have been praising The Body for its mix of teen drama, religious horror, supernatural horror, and religious guilt, as well as how it addresses LGBTQ+ relationships given the show’s time setting and social environment. On the note of The Body’s time setting, some critics have also praised its nostalgia factor.

The Body could have gone down a very cliché route with its religious elements, teen rebellion, and what happens to Grace after the ritual, but instead, Shepherd made sure to keep the audience on their toes in every episode, with twists, reveals, and mysteries. The Body leaves some unanswered questions that further keep the audience engaged after the show is over, and which will be key to season 2’s story if the show is renewed.

The Body is unlike any other teen drama and horror show released this year (and in recent years), offering a unique look into adolescence, guilt, grief, identity, and community.

4 Upcoming Space Operas That Will Compete For Being The Sci-Fi Movie Event Of The Decade, Ranked By Hype

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The world of sci-fi is in a truly promising position right now, with a range of space opera movies on the horizon that hold the potential to be the defining event of the decade for the sci-fi genre, depending on how good they are upon release, and how much they resonate with the masses of people who are already excited for these films.

Throughout much of our on-screen history, space operas have paved the way when it comes to what people expect from sci-fi stories broadly speaking. The likes of Star Wars and Star Trek have done this perhaps most obviously, but cornerstone sci-fi releases like The Fifth Element and Flash Gordon have a cultural footprint that shouldn't be forgotten either, especially since sci-fi fans still flock to watch or rewatch these stories.

However, that isn't the say that space operas are confined solely to the past. Indeed, there are many huge sci-fi films on the horizon that fit into this category, and which look as though they could be some of the next movies that define the genre in a major way on the big screen - and the already colossal fanbases behind all of them only further suggest this will be true for at least some of these upcoming films.

While people tend to think simply of giant robots battling in space when it comes to the Gundam franchise, it has historically had a much deeper level of intergalactic political intrigue and factions in its stories. This warrants its newest film adaptation being regarded as a space opera, and one with considerable hype behind it, given the huge fandom the wider franchise enjoys.

Netflix's description of the film emphasizes the scale and scope of the story the upcoming live-action Gundam movie is set to reveal, describing the plot as seeing the conflict between opposing mech pilots seeing them "pulled into a high-stakes race across the stars that could define the fate of humanity." (via Netflix Tudum).

With decades of intricate lore to delve headfirst into, the likes of stars like Sydney Sweeney set to play central roles in the upcoming 2027 sci-fi movie, and a truly epic story for it on the horizon, Gundam has the potential to kickstart the franchise's on-screen empire in a hugely exciting way depending on how its constructed and subsequently received.

Of course, the Avatar film series has been one of the biggest heavy hitters in all of the movie realm ever since the original released in 2009. As such, the plans for Avatar 4 and Avatar 5 are a big deal to say the least, especially because the scale of the world and story beind told have gotten bigger with every instalment.

Given every Avatar movie thus far has made well over $1 billion - and the first two films grossed well over $2 billion, with the original still being one of the very highest earning movies of all time - it's more likely than not that Avatar 4 will follow suit, so long as it holds up to the standard set visually and in terms of the franchise's overarching story up until now.

With Avatar: Fire and Ash serving to set up much in terms of drama and worldbuilding for the next leg of the journey, it's also easy to see how Avatar 4 could well be one of the strongest entries in the series and sci-fi genre more broadly. Now the franchise has had three movies to set its stage, Avatar 4 has free rein to go all in with its cast of characters and pre-established factions, which bodes seriously well for what happens next.

The next major space opera to hit cinemas is that of Dune: Part Three, which is set to bring the movie adaptation of one of the most famous and acclaimed sci-fi book series of all time to a new fever pitch when it releases in December 2026.

The universe of Dune is one of the most intricate and enrapturing imaginable, with writer Frank Herbert's attention to detail regarding every aspect of the interconnected geopolitcal (or galactic-political) tapestry he weaved leaving the narrative endlessly compelling, and capable of evoking just about every emotional a good story possibly could.

The prior two movie chapters of the latest efforts to adapt Herbert's story into a film epic have been received well both critically and commercially, and have been setting the stage for a climactic and grand third installment. Though there's plenty of room for the Dune movies to continue to adapt the rest of the novels, Dune: Part Three is sure to be a special chapter for the franchise whether this happens or not, since it's already clear it will adapt one of the more integral points of Paul Atreides' story, and the wider narrative consequently.

Though Star Wars did return to the big screen in 2026 with The Mandalorian and Grogu, the upcoming 2027 Star Wars: Starfighter is in some ways more of a return to form, since the 2026 film is more focused on being a feature-length film spinoff of The Mandalorian show to some degree.

Star Wars: Starfighter does mirror some of the original Star Wars movie line's story in a promising way already. Protagonist starfighter pilot and "cynical rogue" Kade finding himself in a galaxy-spanning adventure (via StarWars.com) does seem to mirror the likes of Han Solo, who is also a semi-jaded rogue and pilot who finds himself thrown into various unexpected situations after he agrees to smuggle Luke Skywalker, Obi-Wan Kenobi, R2-D2 and C-3PO off Tatooine.

Star Wars already has the benefit of being arguably the single biggest sci-fi franchise of all time, which is sure to set up Star Wars: Starfighter nicely by itself. Add to that the matter of Ryan Gosling being the movie's central star - following his name becoming even more renowned in the sci-fi film world after the success of Project Hail Mary in 2026 - and it's easy to see how Star Wars: Starfighter could become the biggest space opera movie event of the decade, so long as the cards land just right.

In Khalil Mack's Los Angeles Penthouse, the Ceiling Glows and the Kids Climb the Walls

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Brianna Mack is outnumbered. Her husband is the NFL linebacker Khalil Mack. Their sons, Deuce and Kylo, five and four, run around like the Marvel characters they love. Still, walk through their Los Angeles penthouse and you see Brianna everywhere. The mohair rugs are soft, if not precious, because she wanted the kids to be able to roll around on them. A fluted marble fireplace and a Ferrari Sheppard painting make visitors stop and stare. “That piece looks like Brianna,” says AD PRO Directory interior designer Ryan Saghian. “The confidence, the way the figure is sitting back. She’s got swag. It was made for her.” Brianna built the room around it. “Nice, soft, but not overly feminine,” Brianna says of the balance she struck. “I think that’s life when you’re the only female among three males.”

Khalil with his son on the De Sede sofa. Coffee table by Eternity Modern.

“It’s like a stop-and-think piece,” Brianna Mack says of the Ferrari Sheppard artwork that anchors the entry. Rick Owens ebony stool; marble console by Coco Republic; Ferm Living rug; walls in Portola Roman Clay. Art advisor: Jenna Cesolini.

They were previously living in Newport, California. Khalil was losing four hours a day commuting to the LA Chargers’ facility in El Segundo. Now he’s fifteen minutes from work. Thirty on a day of bad traffic. But Brianna worried about what her sons would sacrifice. Their new home would have no backyard space. “Before we even knew the colors, before we picked out any materials, we knew that was going to be a priority,” she says. The solution: a soundproof playroom with a climbing wall, designed so the boys could be their cherished Marvel characters without disturbing the neighbors below. “We never want to restrain them from being who they are,” Brianna says.

“It’s like their own little mansion in the sky,” Ryan Saghian says. De Sede sofa; armchairs by Amber Lewis for Four Hands; vintage Moroccan rug; antique Dutch library table from Chak Monet Antiques; dried manzanita from Potting Hill; drapery in Fabricut.

The Mack family in the living room. De Sede sofa; walls in Portola Roman Clay.

The Hermès plates were collected from Lapérouse in Paris, a Mack family tradition when traveling. Table and chairs by Eichholtz Los Angeles; chair upholstery by Innovations; pendant by Veneto Luce.

Shearling armchair and ottoman by Amber Lewis for Four Hands; coffee tables by Eternity Modern; rug from Caravan Rugs; drapery in Fabricut; walls in Portola Roman Clay.

The couple hired Saghian, a 34-year-old designer whose clientele runs heavy on athletes and entertainers. He describes Brianna as “the opposite of basic.” His challenge was honoring that without overwhelming Khalil’s minimalism. “The high-fashion elements that are more related to her aesthetic, I introduced in places like artwork, the rugs,” he says. “But the overall architecture of the space I kept as minimal as I possibly could.” Due to his busy schedule, Khalil was often absent during the design process. “He wasn’t in the meetings,” Brianna says, but she knew his taste as well as her own. Saghian, she says, “just got it.” The project, which took two years, involved gutting the place to the studs, starting over with Roman clay walls and hardware-free cabinetry. Touch latches. Finger pulls. Nothing to interrupt the quiet they were seeking.

“No moldings, it’s all Roman clay,” Ryan Saghian says of the walls throughout. A sepia-toned landscape mural by Belarte Studio wraps the formal dining room. Table and chairs by Eichholtz Los Angeles; plaster relief sculpture by Uttermost; Veneto Luce pendant.

While the family has a private chef, Khalil still enjoys cooking for his family. Here, the NFL star prepares a meal while Brianna and their sons gather at the island. Cabinetry by Gefen Builders; Wolf appliances; natural quartzite countertops.

The kitchen’s waterfall-edge quartzite island was chosen after the couple visited stone yards in person. Cognac leather stools with brass bases; cabinetry by Gefen Builders; pendant by Apparatus Studio.

On Khalil’s days off, he cooks breakfast. The boys eat at a round dining table with a shattered-glass finish, leather bar stools in cognac around it. A wall of plates from memorable restaurants they’ve visited, Lapérouse in Paris among them. The kitchen was designed to be durable. Two young sons and an NFL player’s appetite will test any surface. But when morning light fills the room, Brianna takes it in. “This is beautiful,” she says. “This is our space.”

“When you’re looking at these beautiful textures, it does affect your mood,” Brianna Mack says. A Monica Ord painting hangs above a travertine pedestal in the hallway; walls throughout are finished in Portola Roman Clay.

“It just has this zen to it,” Brianna Mack says of the home’s atmosphere. The powder room’s custom stone vanity is carved from a single slab; walls in Portola Roman Clay; backlit mirror.

“He loves his wife—I mean, he’s obsessed with her—and he loves his kids. I’ve never seen a guy like that,” Ryan Saghian says. Brianna Mack with Deuce and Kylo in the primary suite’s sitting area. Sofa by J.Alexander Furniture; artwork by William McLure; Moroccan alpaca-mohair rug from Caravan Rugs.

The primary suite’s sitting area. Sofa by J.Alexander Furniture in S.Harris fabric; swivel chairs and coffee table by Eichholtz Los Angeles; pendant by Vakkerlight in alabaster and saddle leather; Moroccan alpaca-mohair rug from Caravan Rugs; artwork by William McLure; florals by Goodroots LA.

“This is a guy who comes home exhausted not only mentally but physically,” Saghian says of Khalil. So they knocked down walls and rebuilt what was necessary: hyperbaric chamber, massage space. “I needed a spot like this to just relax and recover,” Khalil says. Brianna took a room nearby for hair and makeup. And when the work is done, there’s a screening room that includes a pit-style sofa, a candy bar, and a glowing fiber-optic ceiling. Saghian borrowed the last detail from a Rolls-Royce. “I look at [game] film in there if I need to,” Khalil says. “But it’s more fun to watch movies as a family there.”

A black marble soaking tub, so heavy it required building approval before it could enter the elevator, sits between dual rain showers. Checkerboard floor in black and cream marble.

The primary bath’s checkerboard floor in black and cream marble anchors a floating travertine vanity. Cabinetry by Gefen Builders.

A sculptural travertine lounger curves beneath an arched niche in the primary bath’s steam room.

The playroom was a priority from the first meeting. Beneath the cork and rubber underlayment is half an inch of soundproofing. Build-out by Gefen Builders; padded walls and drapery in Fabricut; pendant by Vakkerlight.

"They love to be Spider-Man climbing,” Brianna Mack says. The playroom was converted from a guest room, with soundproof walls and a half-inch rubber layer beneath the hardwood. Build-out by Gefen Builders; padded walls by Phillip Jeffries; drapery in Fabricut; pendant by Vakkerlight.

In the boy’s bedroom, the bed wall is entirely custom, with twin beds set into niches and recessed shelves; footballs from their father line the opposite wall. Bedding by Parachute; chest by Pottery Barn; rug by Nordik Knots.

“We really own this space,” Brianna says. “A place for our boys to feel loved and to have a sense of identity.” She had hoped for something good. She got more than that. “It superseded what I thought it would be.” Some mornings, before the noise starts, just as the light begins to fill their home, she looks around and takes it in. “I’m falling more in love with the space,” Brianna says. A life they built from the studs up.

“It’s basically one huge wall-to-wall pit that the whole family can get on to watch TV,” Ryan Saghian says. The room, converted from the den, features padded walls by Phillip Jeffries; motorized drapery in Fabricut; custom modular seating; and a fiber-optic ceiling inspired by a Rolls-Royce.

Hong Kong: Fire Anniversary Underscores Growing Repression

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Click to expand Image A memorial near the site of the deadly fire at the residential complex at Wang Fuk Court in Tai Po, Hong Kong, December 2, 2025. © 2025 Ng Han Guan/AP Photo

(London) – The Hong Kong government should ensure meaningful accountability for the devastating fire that killed 168 people one year ago and end its suppression of peaceful criticism, Human Rights Watch said today.

On November 26, 2025, Hong Kong’s deadliest fire in decades engulfed seven residential high-rise buildings at Wang Fuk Court, Tai Po. The authorities initiated an independent investigation and arrested three dozen people implicated in crimes. The Housing Minister resigned. But the authorities have also threatened and arbitrarily arrested those engaging in criticism and activism concerning the fire.

“The Hong Kong government responded to the horrific Wang Fuk Court fire by using Beijing’s tactics of shutting out the voices of the victims and promising an elusive accountability,” said Elaine Pearson, Asia director at Human Rights Watch. “The authorities need to recognize that the only way to prevent similar tragedies in the future is to allow people to speak out safely and hold those responsible fully accountable.”

The authorities have engaged in apparent acts of repression of free expression in the weeks ahead of the anniversary:

The media reported in November that the police made inquiries with Catholic churches planning masses to commemorate victims of the fire.A church in Tai Po subsequently removed references to the fire from its announcement and instead described the service as a mass for “the brothers and sisters who died in the past year;” hundreds nevertheless attended.A priest at another Catholic church posted on Facebook that three police officers had inquired about a planned memorial mass marking the anniversary, even though the church had not publicly announced the service.On November 22, a group working with migrant workers who survived the fire cancelled a book launch and drama event scheduled ahead of the anniversary, citing “unforeseen circumstances.” On November 23, the online news platform Boom News said it would remove some videos featuring interviews with survivors, quoting the whistleblower Edward Snowden on “censorship and fear.” The Boom News founder, Tang Ho-wing, had been arrested in September for alleged “sedition” for reporting on another politically sensitive anniversary.

Police Commissioner Joe Chow said police routinely asked organizers about upcoming public events, while decisions on whether to proceed “rested with the organizers themselves.”

Amid continued restrictions on freedom of expression and peaceful assembly in Hong Kong, Hong Kong communities around the world have planned commemorations of the fire.

Deep concerns surrounding the 2026 anniversary follow a year-long government crackdown on criticism, reporting, and civil society-organizing related to the fire. Immediately following the fire, hundreds of people gathered near Wang Fuk Court to donate and distribute supplies to victims, but as the volunteer effort grew, police told them to leave and government workers took over the site.

Authorities targeted people calling for accountability. Just four days after the fire, national security police reportedly arrested a university student, Miles Kwan, for “seditious intent” after he initiated an online petition calling for an independent investigation into the fire, and two others on suspicion of “inciting hatred against the government.”

Hong Kong and Beijing authorities subsequently accused “foreign forces” and “anti-China destabilizing forces” of using the fire to “incite hatred against the government.” Within hours of these announcements, two prominent online commentators, Ellie Yuen and Hailey Cheng, who had been frequently quoted in foreign media, said they would stop reporting on the fire.

National security police also arrested the online commentator Wong On-yin in December after he planned to hold a news conference about the Wang Fuk Court fire and discussed on YouTube his subsequent questioning by police. The authorities prosecuted him for allegedly obstructing a national security investigation and publishing “seditious material.” He remains in custody awaiting a verdict in January 2027.

Between April and May 2026, the authorities barred Wang Fuk Court residents from displaying banners from their homes or being accompanied by journalists as they were briefly permitted to return to their apartments to retrieve belongings.

Also in May, the authorities arrested the former Wang Fuk Court owners’ committee member Kong Cheung-fat and his wife on allegations of fraud and money laundering. Kong had joined other owners in seeking a meeting to discuss insurance and other post-fire issues. While out on bail, he said he would no longer give interviews or comment publicly. Some residents said his arrest made them more cautious about speaking publicly.

An independent committee, set up by Hong Kong Chief Executive John Lee, published a report on October 29 that found wide-ranging regulatory failures across four government bodies, including inadequate oversight of renovation work and fire safety, weaknesses in building maintenance regulation, and supervisory failures by contractors and other professionals. That day, Lee and senior officials formally apologized to the victims of the fire and their families while promising to implement “systemic reforms.” Housing Secretary Winnie Ho later stepped down from office while the authorities arrested 35 people in connection with the fire and possible corruption in the renovation project.

“An apology and promises of reform are no substitute for ensuring that people can freely question and criticize the authorities,” Pearson said. “The Hong Kong government should act on the independent committee’s recommendations and provide adequate compensation and other support for victims and their families.”

Chris Evans And The Rock’s Overlooked Christmas Movie With An 89% Audience Score Is Streaming On Prime Video

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Chris Evans and Dwayne “The Rock” Johnson are two of the most famous actors working today. As such, both have been in seemingly countless huge projects, from the Avengers movies for Evans to the Fast and Furious franchise for The Rock. Because of this, both actors have appeared in movies that, comparatively speaking, have fallen through the cracks.

That’s certainly the case for Red One, the Jake Kasdan-directed action Christmas film from Amazon, which is currently available for streaming on Prime Video. Red One, which was written by Chris Morgan (who penned Fast and Furious: Tokyo Drift), tells the story of the North Pole’s head of security as he teams up with a well-respected hacker in order to find Santa Claus before Christmas Eve, as he’s been kidnapped.

Red One wasn’t a huge hit with critics, earning a 29% score on Rotten Tomatoes. The film also failed to earn back its production budget, taking in just over $180 million off a $250 million cost. Despite that, Red One proved to be much more popular with fans, as the film has an 89% audience score on Rotten Tomatoes and consistently sits near the top of the charts on Prime Video each holiday season.

As the holidays are quickly approaching, many are looking to streaming services to watch a cozy movie this season. There are, of course, the certified Christmas classics, such as Home Alone, A Christmas Story, and How the Grinch Stole Christmas, but movies like Red One are also deserving of a watch around this time of year, especially if you’re looking for something new and fresh this time around.

As stated, Red One follows the North Pole head of security, Callum Drift, who’s played by The Rock. Drift is a loyal servant to Santa Claus, who’s portrayed by JK Simmons, and the two have worked together for hundreds of years. Red One tells the story of how that centuries-long relationship nearly falls apart due to an evil scheme to capture Father Christmas just before his busiest day of the year.

This naturally sends the entirety of the North Pole into a panic. With Santa Claus gone, it becomes a real possibility that there will be no Christmas at all, leaving millions of children all around the world without gifts under their tree. Drift, however, is determined not to let that happen, and is willing to do whatever it takes to bring Santa Claus back as quickly as possible.

Through the help of Mythological Oversight and Restoration Authority (MORA), an organization that helps to keep the peace between human and mythological societies, Drift is able to determine that the kidnappers discovered Santa’s location through the work of an infamous hacker named Jack O’Malley, played by Chris Evans.

Despite his involvement, O’Malley had no idea what he was hired to do, and instead just completed the task as he would with any other job. Drift then recruits O’Malley, who has long since been a skeptic of the existence of Santa Claus, to help bring back Simmons’ character, with time quickly running down.

Many aspects of Red One were criticized by critics, but The Rock and Chris Evans both give strong performances in their respective roles. As such, there are still reasons to watch or rewatch it now on Prime Video.

One of the most criticized aspects of Red One was the clear franchise set up that the film integrated throughout. An entire world was being established, showing not just the North Pole, but the South Pole (with Krampus himself) and other mythological locations and figures as well. This bothered some, who said that it made Red One feel too much like a clear attempt to build a Christmas-themed cinematic universe rather than simply making a good movie.

However, on the other hand, a lot of Red One’s world building actually does work. The presence of Krampus, for example, helps to make the world feel truly lived in, meaning that it brought more to the movie than just an attempt at future franchise success. And, of course, Red One did have plenty of franchise potential, but it doesn’t look as if that will ever come to fruition. Despite that, audiences can still enjoy Red One on Prime Video this holiday season.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Illustrations of Moral Dissonance

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 25, 2026 | Reviewed by Lybi Ma

The Trojan War was in its tenth year. A soothsayer had foretold the Greeks that they would win the war only by retrieving the famed archer, Philoctetes, whose bow was from Heracles and whose arrows never missed their mark. The major problem, though, was that Odysseus and his men had abandoned Philoctetes on their way to Troy those many years earlier. On a stop before they reached Troy, Philoctetes had inadvertently wandered into a sacred area and been bitten by a snake. He developed a wound so suppurative and foul-smelling and cried out in such agony that no one could stand to be with him, and Odysseus ordered his shipmates to drop him off on the deserted island of Lemnos (Sophocles; Heaney, 1961).

For all that time, Philoctetes, miserable and alone, suffered with a virulent, gangrenous, and oozing wound that did not heal, "an insatiable sickness" (Sophocles). All the while, he nursed a grudge against Odysseus and his men, and he was obsessed with the betrayal (Lansky, 2003; Wilson, 2018).

There are passing references to this myth in both Homer's Iliad and Odyssey, as well as in Ovid's Metamorphoses and Virgil's Aeneid, but the main extant source is Sophocles’ play, Philoctetes. The story has also been immortalized in many works of art.

Odysseus — the clever manipulator and far more devious than depicted in Christopher Nolan's recent film, The Odyssey — devised an elaborate plan: He and some of his men would land on Lemnos with Neoptolemus, the young son of Achilles, and trick Philoctetes into coming, with his magic bow, back with them to Troy.

Commanded by Odysseus and threatened with punishment for treason if he would not comply, Neoptolemus was to befriend Philoctetes and explain that he, too, had a grievance against Odysseus. He had reason, therefore, to abandon the Greeks and take Philoctetes home, all the while plotting to bring him to Troy.

But Neoptolemus was a young man of character: "It is not in my nature to practice treachery...Don't you think it shameful to lie?" he asks. "It goes against all I was ever brought up to believe," he adds. (Heaney). "Not if the lie brings deliverance...Troy can be taken only with his bow," responds Odysseus.

Neoptolemus ultimately agrees to accept his commander's duplicitous plan that includes the possibility of stealing the bow and leaving Philoctetes to starve.

Seeing the extraordinary anguish of Philoctetes moves Neoptolemus, as he says, "My heart is bursting with grief at your pain." With renewed courage and honor, he refuses to acquiesce to Odysseus' deception (Segal, 1995). "What is disgusting is when a man abandons his own true nature and acts shamefully," says Neoptolemus.

Though Neoptolemus confesses his real intentions to Philoctetes, it will take a deus ex machina in the form of the appearance of Heracles to convince Philoctetes that his destiny is to accompany Odysseus, Neoptolemus, and the men to Troy. Philoctetes, as foretold, will have his wound healed and will be "hailed as the bravest of all the Greeks" with the fall of Troy: Philoctetes will kill Paris, and Neoptolemus will kill King Priam (Wilson).

Sophocles’ play depicts conflicts between the individual and society (Segal, 1995) and poignantly illustrates the effects of chronic, unremitting disease for which someone is ostracized and abandoned. Most significantly, it reflects the high moral cost of compromising one's integrity (Rangell, 1974), as Neoptolemus had, and experiencing moral dissonance (ter Heide and Olff, 2023; VanderWeele et al, 2025), or moral injury (Shay, 1991; 1994; 2002; 2011; Griffin et al, 2019; Litz, 2025; Litz and Walker, 2025).

Psychoanalytically, this compromise of integrity results from a conflict between the ego, the rational part of the brain, and the superego, one's conscience (Rangell, 1974).

Jonathan Shay is credited with introducing the term moral injury in the context of the military. It is a betrayal of what’s right and a “shrinkage of the moral and social horizon.” Moral hypocrisy occurs when immoral actions occur in otherwise moral people (Naso, 2006).

Shay compared Homeric soldiers with American soldiers: Homeric soldiers respected, even honored their enemy, without derogatory name-calling or dehumanizing them, as was commonly encouraged by some in the American armed forces.

Shay thought of moral injury as psychological wound contamination, a kind of leadership malpractice. There are three parts to his definition: there is a betrayal of what’s right, a high-stakes situation, and someone who holds legitimate authority over a person (2011). Moral distress results from "culturally imbued shared and internalized values" (Griffin et al.).

Others have extended the use of the term to situations beyond the military (Litz, 2025), though there is no consensus definition or gold standard of measurement (Litz and Walker, 2025). Further, research suffers from small sample sizes and even disagreement about what is a potentially morally injurious event (Griffin et al., 2019).

“The human story is replete with individuals, groups, and nations wittingly and unwittingly perpetrating cruel and pernicious acts of inhumanity against others." Psychological consequences ensue. “People can be consumed and haunted by these inhumane acts” (Litz and Walker). Moral injury now includes acts of commission or omission, being the victim of, or bearing witness to acts “that transgress deeply held moral beliefs and expectations” (Litz and Walker).

Situations that can evoke moral injury include the My Lai Massacre in March 1968, where American soldiers slaughtered at least 300 unarmed Vietnamese civilians (Borch, 2018).

The torture and abuse of prisoners by Americans at Abu Ghraib in Iraq in 2003 is another example.

Though the effects of moral injury can overlap with PTSD, the two are distinct. PTSD is often fear-based and involves flashbacks, exaggerated startle reflex, memory loss, nightmares, and insomnia (Griffin et al.).

The syndrome of moral injury, in distinction, does not require a life-threatening event (Litz, 2024). It is "persistent moral distress" (VanderWeel et al.), more likely involving feelings of guilt, shame, anger, anhedonia, social isolation (Griffin et al.), loss of trust in others or institutions, failure to forgive oneself or others, and loss of meaning in life (VanderWeel et al.).

The damage from moral injury can be irreparable and exceptionally difficult to treat. Cognitive-behavioral coping approaches, such as habituation and reappraisal, often fail to relieve the distress (Griffin et al.).

How To Complete The Final Flush In GTA 6

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You can get a free stamina upgrade in GTA 6 by helping out a conspiracy theorist, but there's a dark easter egg to uncover after you're done, too.

I'm convinced that Lucia is a clone in Grand Theft Auto 6 — at least, in my playthrough. If you happen on a little side quest called The Final Flush, where you help a conspiracy theorist uncover the secret machinations of Big Sewage, things get a little weird. Weird enough that yes, I really am convinced Lucia is a clone. Just don't let the FIB hear me posting about that on Snapmatic, yeah?

In all seriousness, this is one of those oddities in GTA 6 where you don't really understand what's happening, things veer a little X-Files, and then you're handed a permanent stamina upgrade for your troubles as though you haven't been wading through literal crap.

Like all good encounters, you start The Final Flush by scanning a QR code on a handwritten poster by a toilet that screams "STOP! DON'T FLUSH." I also found the same poster in the storage compartment of a pawn shop, but if you want a reliable way to begin the mission instead of going on a Vice City crime spree until you happen across one, head to the north of Corsair Key in Mariana County and enter the only building on the entire island - the toilet.

Use the WaiNK IT app to scan the QR code (#NOFLUSHERS) and you'll get a message asking you to reply "TRUTH" to join the movement. If you decide to embrace your inner tinfoil hat, you'll get a follow-up message telling you to meet the sender at Frank's Waffles in Vice City, whereupon arriving you'll need to repeat the code: 00-00-02.

Drive to Frank's Waffles, and you'll see an older man muttering to himself at a booth by the toilets. This is the colorful Dr. Merle, a conspiracy theorist with an ingenious plan: swallow a tracker, poop it out, and follow it to the source.

Of course, as Lucia rightfully points out (you can also do this mission with Jason), he could've just thrown the tracker in the toilet and saved himself the trouble. But he waffles about how Node is too smart for that, and that he needs to be stealthier.

If you return to Frank's Waffles and check the toilet later, well, I decided not to include the screenshot.

The point is, supposedly, Big Sewage is harvesting Leonidan DNA through their poop to make clones of its citizens, and Dr. Merle wants to get to the bottom of it.

Immediately after depositing the tracker, Dr. Merle will ask you to drive his van as he yells directions at you. You can take a guess at where the tracker in the sewage pipes leads — the sewage plant.

Fast and Furious fans have an extra special easter egg to find in GTA 6: Paul Walker's Mitsubishi Eclipse from the first film.

Just make sure you have the compass enabled, as you won't get a trail on your mini-map to follow, only sporadic directions from Dr. Merle as he tells you to drive east, north, and in whatever direction the pipes are leading.

Eventually, you will get close enough that a waypoint appears on the beach. Park the van on the coast and follow Dr. Merle on foot along the fence, where he'll start lockpicking a gate to get you inside.

Dr. Merle will ask you to keep watch while he very slowly works a lock that Lucia and Jason could pick in seconds. You can't really do much though, because it's a chain-link fence that security can easily see through.

So, when a van pulls up asking what exactly you're doing, select 'Defuse' to tell the worker you're fixing the gate. They won't believe you, but it will buy you enough time to get inside and start sneaking about.

Once inside, head to the pink area marked on your map. You can either talk to the workers and lie that you're electricians, or sneak past them through the double doors into the adjacent building (like with all interiors, it's marked by a darker shade of blue).

Here, Dr. Merle will break down and ramble about how close he got to finding out the truth, only to be thwarted by concrete, as the tracker is right below your feet. This is where things take a turn. His poop-laden tracker isn't just lurking in underground pipes, there's a secret facility.

Head to the balcony overseeing the room where there's a control panel with three buttons. Turn all the lights on the panel green (press the bottom two) and then interact with the main lever. Right where Dr. Merle is sitting, a secret staircase will be revealed leading to a strange chamber.

Naturally, like with any conspiracy theorist worth his salt, Dr. Merle puts his idea to the test and climbs into the chamber. It quickly fills with a strange, viscous green liquid, and he asks you — Jason or Lucia — to press the button on the control panel and quite literally flush him. Do it.

One of GTA 6's best easter eggs comes with some very unique accessories. Here's how to get them.

The room blares with the sound of the agitated water authority warning you that the police are en route and that the chamber is toxic and should not be entered. But if you want a permanent stamina upgrade, I'm afraid you have to get inside and follow Dr. Merle's example.

You'll enter first-person while it fills with that strange green liquid again, and bizarrely, you wake up outside your safehouse, unclear how you got there, stamina upgrade in hand.

Okay, I'm not only convinced that my Lucia is a clone. I have proof. And this is where the guide takes a turn. After completing The Big Flush, you can find a disturbing little easter egg revealing what exactly happened in the chamber.

Head north to Lummox County and travel to the eastern island. Make sure you don't stumble onto Hyde Island, or you'll end up being hunted for sport.

Near the tip, there's a sealed bunker. Enter the code '3612' to open the door, and head on inside. There's no reward here, just a disturbing revelation that will change how you see the rest of the game.

Through the bunker and down a ladder, you'll see three corpses — so long as you've done the mission first: Dr. Merle, whoever you put in the chamber, and an unknown bald man (I checked his face, it isn't Jason).

Naturally, as you can see in the video embedded above, I brought Jason along so he could find out that the Lucia at home is really a clone, which makes every single text and interaction feel just a little bit off. But hey, a free stamina upgrade is a free stamina upgrade.

Or... maybe these are discarded clones. Who's to say?

Leonida's zoo is one of GTA 6's most impressive locations. Here's how to see it for yourself.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Ezra Frech Reacts to Prosthetic Falling Off Mid-Dance During DWTS

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Before Ezra Frech and Daniella Karagach were crowned the Dancing With the Stars season 35 winners, they seamlessly navigated Frech’s prosthetic leg falling off mid-dance.

There was a moment Ezra Frech didn’t have a leg to stand on—but he had a ballroom supporting him.

After the Paralympian’s prosthetic leg came off during his freestyle dance alongside partner Daniella Karagach on the Dancing With the Stars Nov. 24 finale, he revealed what went through his head before seamlessly reattaching the limb with the help of his partner as well as pro Alan Bersten.

“The leg just wasn't on correctly, and I felt it,” he told Variety following the episode, which saw him and Karagach crowned as the season’s winners. “Sometimes it'll readjust and I'll be okay. And so, as I was running up the steps, I'm like, ‘There's a 50 percent chance my leg will resuction and I'll be okay.’ And that wasn't the case.”

Nevertheless, he was ready to do what had to be done to finish the dance. After all, it was a moment he and Karagach had long been preparing for.

“I was just like, ‘Okay, I'm gonna do the rest of the dance on one leg. Like I'll do the entire thing on one leg,’” the 21-year-old continued. “She's drilled in me since the beginning that no matter what happens, we keep pushing through. You never give up, and you fight for it. And in this case, regardless of the circumstance, I was gonna do whatever I can to fight for it.”

Thanks to the quick thinking of his pro, however, Frech was able to get a leg back up the competition—literally. During a brief pause in the dance, Karagach appeared to yell something to her partner as she handed him his prosthetic, allowing Frech a few beats to reattach the limb.

“I heard her say, ‘Put your leg on,’” he recalled, “and I was like, ‘Yes, okay, yeah, good idea. I'll put it on right now.’”

While Karagach had the utmost faith in her partner’s ability to persevere, she knew  taking the time to reattach the limb was worth it.

“I was just like, there is no other place in the routine that he can do this right now, and he cannot do the whole routine on one leg,” she explained. “So, I was like, ‘Just put your leg on.’ And it's not like—we had one little trick in there that he did on one, like a 540. It was really impressive. It was really great. But nothing's better than him just clicking on his leg properly and continuing on with the dance. I was like, ‘This is our only shot to do that.’”

The moment was so adrenaline inducing, however, that Frech admitted he didn’t quite remember how his prosthetic made it back onto his body.

“Did somebody give me the leg? Because I blacked out,” he asked aloud, to which Karagach confirmed she did. “You gave me the leg? Really?”

Thankfully, those few seconds were the only thing Frech needed.

“I clicked it on,” he said, “and when I put it on the second time. I was like, ‘Ooh it's on now, we're good.’”

And good he was, with he and Karagach crowned the season 35 winners by the end of the episode—an accomplishment as good as gold for Frech.

"This is the most special moment of my life," he told the audience. "Thank you, everybody, for voting. Thank you for believing in us. Oh my God."

For a look back at the other dancing pairs Frech and Karagach had to outstep all the way to the Len Goodman Mirrorball Trophy, read on.

The 10 Things I Hate About You star had her last dance with her pro partner during Nov. 2's Disney Night.

The celebrity chef couldn't cha-cha-chop away the competition and was eliminated during the Sept. 22 episode.

What did you miss on Glee? The actor taking his musical talents to the dance floor.

Sadly, the former '90s teen heartthrob did not MMMBop his way to the top of the leaderboard on season 35. He and his pro partner were axed from the competition on Sept. 29.

The Fresh Prince of Bel-Air actress will be bringing her fresh moves to the dance floor.

Twenty years after starring in Step Up, the dancer stepped up—and out—when she got cut from the competition in a shocking Nov. 10 elimination.

The 2026 Olympic figure skater will take her skills from the ice to the ballroom.

The Team USA Paralympic track and field athlete will show off his athleticism on DWTS.

Though her sister Brooks Nader was the fifth star to be eliminated on season 33, the Love Thy Nader star said goodbye to the competition after her debut episode on Sept. 16.

The comedian, also known as Fibula on social media, is ready to earn more applause on DWTS.

The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives star followed his wife Whitney Leavitt's footsteps after her season 34 appearance, but he bid farwell to the season 35 competition during the Sept. 15 premiere.

Though the Bachelor Nation star traded in roses for rumbas, he was sent home on Oct. 27 in a double elimination.

The Love Island U.K. alum is hoping her love for DWTS doesn't betray her as she manifested this experience.

"I have a vision board and I redo it every year," she told E! News in May. "This year is the first year that I stayed at home by myself on New Year's Eve. I did not go out at all. I made myself a bowl of pasta and a glass of red wine and I redid my vision board for the year. Dancing With the Stars was already on it from the year before. However, I added five more Dancing With the Stars on the board."

In the world of streaming, with a variety of platforms to choose from, both subscription-based and free, Tubi continues to stand out. Tubi has positioned itself as the best free streaming platform (with ads, of course) thanks to its extensive offer of movies and TV shows, including original productions.
